### [1] [Long-Integration Magnetar Burst Observatory (LIMBO): instrument summary and early FRB rate constraints]()
*McCauley, Darby,Parsons, Aaron,Liu, Wei,Lu, Wenbin,Wright, Dirk,Werthimer, Dan*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: LIMBO是一个实时射电暂现源探测pipeline，采用4.3米口径碟形天线监测银河系磁星，对SGR 1935+2154进行了833小时观测，获得12个候选FRB探测，并推断出流量密度积范围为10至$10^6$ Jy ms的累积率–流量密度积幂律斜率。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 该研究旨在验证采用小型、实时连续监测的方法能否有效搜索来自银河系磁星（如SGR 1935+2154）的色散快速射电暴（FRB），并据此约束其类FRB事件率及率–流量密度积分布。

Method: 摘要说明：LIMBO采用4.3米碟形天线与双极化馈源，以$1460 \,\mathrm{MHz}$为中心、$125 \,\mathrm{MHz}$有效带宽，通过实时处理pipeline在相加偏振上进行色散暂现源搜索，探测触发电压数据转储；基于校准灵敏度测量、合成信号注入与恢复测试及蟹状脉冲星脉冲探测评估灵敏度；2023年5-8月对SGR 1935+2154进行833小时后续观测。

Result: 摘要报告LIMBO对流量密度积$\gtrsim43\,\mathrm{Jy\,ms}$的暂现源敏感；观测中获12个候选FRB探测，若为真实事件则测得类FRB事件率$R(\ge65\,\mathrm{Jy\,ms})=112.3^{+81.3}_{-54.5}\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$和$R(\ge130\,\mathrm{Jy\,ms})=17.7^{+40.8}_{-15.1}\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$；结合先前报道事件，在10至$10^6\,\mathrm{Jy\,ms}$范围内推断累积率幂律斜率$\alpha=-0.60^{+0.24}_{-0.28}$。

Conclusion: 作者认为这些观测证明了连续实时监测银河系磁星的能力，并确立了LIMBO作为探测银河系FRB的有效仪器。除非摘要明确指出候选事件与真正FRB事件率的直接等同关系，摘要未说明其与更广义磁星FRB物理的转移性解读。

Abstract: The Long-Integration Magnetar Burst Observatory (LIMBO) is a real-time radio transient detection pipeline designed to search for dispersed fast radio bursts (FRBs) from Galactic magnetars. LIMBO employs a 4.3-m dish with a dual-polarization feed to continuously monitor a <inline-formula><tex-math>$125 \,\mathrm{MHz}$</tex-math></inline-formula> effective band centred at <inline-formula><tex-math>$1460 \,\mathrm{MHz}$</tex-math></inline-formula>. A real-time processing pipeline performs a search for dispersed transients on the summed polarizations, with detections triggering dumps of buffered voltage data to disc. Based on calibrated sensitivity measurements, synthetic signal-injection and recovery tests, and successful detection of pulses from the Crab Pulsar, we determine that LIMBO is sensitive to radio transients with fluences <inline-formula><tex-math>$\gtrsim 43 \,\mathrm{Jy \, ms}$</tex-math></inline-formula>. Between May and August 2023, LIMBO conducted 833 h of follow-up observations of the Galactic magnetar SGR 1935+2154, yielding 12 candidate FRB detections. If these events are true, we measure FRB-like event rates from SGR 1935+2154 of <inline-formula><tex-math>$R(\ge 65 \, \mathrm{Jy \, ms}) = 112.3^{+81.3}_{-54.5} \, \mathrm{yr}^{-1}$</tex-math></inline-formula> and <inline-formula><tex-math>$R(\ge 130 \, \mathrm{Jy \, ms}) = 17.7^{+40.8}_{-15.1} \,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$</tex-math></inline-formula>. Combining these results with previously reported FRBs-like from SGR 1935+2154, we infer a cumulative rate--fluence power-law slope of <inline-formula><tex-math>$\alpha =-0.60^{+0.24}_{-0.28}$</tex-math></inline-formula> in the fluence range between 10 and <inline-formula><tex-math>$10^6\rm \, Jy \, ms$</tex-math></inline-formula>. These observations demonstrate the capability of continuous, real-time monitoring of Galactic magnetars and establish LIMBO as an effective instrument for detecting Galactic FRBs.

</details>


### [2] [Unveiling the spectral morphological division of fast radio bursts with CHIME/FRB Catalog 2]()
*Sun, Wan-Peng,Cao, Yin-Long,Zhang, Yong-Kun,Zhang, Ji-Guo,Liu, Xiaohui,Li, Yichao,Zhang, Fu-Wen,Hou, Wan-Ting,Zhang, Jing-Fei,Zhang, Xin*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 利用第二份CHIME/FRB目录，通过结合UMAP和基于密度聚类的无监督机器学习，发现FRB主要按频谱形态分为两个稳健簇，并识别出一个类似非重复暴发的宽带、更短、更亮的非典型重复暴亚类；结果支持重复与非重复FRB的物理联系。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: FRB通常被分类为重复暴源和表观非重复暴源，但该区别是否反映本质上不同的物理种群仍不确定。本研究旨在通过对第二份CHIME/FRB目录进行多维参数空间分析，揭示FRB种群的内在结构，以探讨两类源之间是否存在物理联系。

Method: 利用第二份CHIME/FRB目录数据，应用无监督机器学习框架，结合均匀流形逼近与投影（UMAP）与基于密度的聚类，在多维参数空间中对FRB种群进行结构分析，并对分类方案的召回率进行评估。摘要未说明具体观测频段、时间跨度及样本数量。

Result: FRB主要被分离为两个以频谱形态为主的稳健聚类：一类为窄带、持续较长，另一类为宽带、暴发时标较短，证实了基于第一份目录的发现；该分类对已知重复暴召回率为0.94。重复暴内部存在稳定的宽带、更短、更亮的非典型亚类。宽带非重复暴的色散量系统性更高（约200 pc cm<SUP>-3</SUP>），各向同性光度约为重复FRB的10倍。

Conclusion: 作者认为，在不引入灾难性前身星情景的情况下，重复与非重复FRB在频谱形态、色散量和光度上的差异可由仪器灵敏度极限和距离相关选择效应自然解释，为两类FRB之间可能的物理联系提供新的统计学证据。根据摘要无法判断该方法对脉冲星、磁星或FRB计时等议题的转移性。

Abstract: Fast radio bursts (FRBs) are commonly classified into repeating and apparently nonrepeating sources, yet whether this distinction reflects intrinsically different physical populations remains uncertain. Using the Second CHIME/FRB Catalog, we apply an unsupervised machine learning framework combining uniform manifold approximation and projection (UMAP) with density-based clustering to investigate the intrinsic structure of the FRB population in a multi-dimensional parameter space. We find that FRBs are primarily separated into two robust clusters dominated by spectral morphology. One cluster is characterized by narrowband emission and longer durations, while the other exhibits relatively broadband spectra and shorter burst timescales, confirming previous findings based on the first CHIME/FRB catalog. This classification scheme achieves a recall of 0.94 for known repeaters. Within the repeating population, we further identify a stable subclass of atypical repeaters that are broadband, shorter in duration, and more luminous, resembling nonrepeating bursts. Furthermore, broadband nonrepeaters exhibit systematically higher dispersion measures (by approximately 200 pc cm<SUP>-3</SUP>) and isotropic luminosities approximately an order of magnitude larger than those of repeating FRBs. Without invoking catastrophic progenitor scenarios, these differences are naturally explained by instrumental sensitivity limits and distance-dependent selection effects. Our results provide new statistical evidence for a physical connection between repeating and nonrepeating FRBs.

</details>


### [3] [A bayesian determination of the Hubble constant using 126 fast radio bursts]()
*Piratova-Moreno, Eduard Fernando*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 本文利用126个已确认宿主星系的快速射电暴（FRB）并采用贝叶斯方法估计哈勃常数H₀，在区分重复类型的整个群体模型中得到最可靠结果H₀ = 71.36 ⁺³.⁵⁹₋₃.₉₄ km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹，并用500个合成FRB展示了可作为缓解哈勃张力之替代方法的潜力。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 本文旨在应对哈勃常数各种测量值之间的分歧，即早期与晚期宇宙测量间约5σ不一致的哈勃张力问题。快速射电暴（FRB）通过色散量对哈勃常数敏感，为缓解这一张力提供了一种替代途径，因此作者试图用该方法估计H₀。

Method: 作者汇编了126个具有已确认宿主星系的FRB数据库，依据IllustrisTNG模拟将其分为三种类型。采用贝叶斯方法在七个宿主模型中估计H₀；其中三个模型宿主贡献与z无关，三个与z相关并仅适用于相应重复子样本，第七个区分每个FRB的重复类型并应用于整个群体。同时考虑YMW16与NE2001两种银河系模型，并使用500个合成FRB目录进行检验。

Result: 对区分重复类型的整个群体模型得到最可靠结果H₀ = 71.36 ⁺³.⁵⁹₋₃.₉₄ km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹。YMW16模型在不同宿主星系假设下始终给出高于NE2001模型的H₀值，差值从+1.12到+13.24 km s⁻¹ Mpc⁻¹。各重复类型得到的H₀值不合理。500个合成FRB带来相对观测值2.6%的偏差，但统计精度从5.5%降至1.2%。

Conclusion: 作者认为FRB是缓解哈勃张力的可行替代方法，500个合成FRB能在仅引入2.6%偏差的情况下将统计精度从5.5%提升到1.2%，显示出巨大潜力。每种重复类型所得的H₀值不合理，表明这些子样本可能仍需进一步建模。根据摘要无法判断本论文结果与脉冲星、磁星计时等的直接关联。

Abstract: The persistent discrepancy between the various measured values of the Hubble constant is one of the most interesting problems in modern cosmology. This problem, in which a discrepancy of approximately 5<inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:mi>σ</mml:mi> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> arises between measurements of the early and late universe, is known as the Hubble Tension. On the other hand, Fast Radio Bursts (FRBs) are high-energy, transient extragalactic phenomena that are sensitive to the Hubble constant through their dispersion measure and therefore offer an alternative approach to alleviating this tension. In this work, we compiled a database of 126 FRBs with confirmed host galaxies, classified into three types based on IllustrisTNG simulations. We used a Bayesian approach to estimate <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:msub> <mml:mrow> <mml:mi>H</mml:mi> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mn>0</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msub> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> across seven host models. Three models treat the host contribution as independent of <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:mi>z</mml:mi> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula>; three others treat it as dependent on <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:mi>z</mml:mi> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula>, reflecting the repeating nature of FRB, and apply only to the corresponding subpopulation. The seventh is applied to the entire FRB population, and each FRB is assigned the appropriate host model. The most reliable result was obtained for the model that considers the entire population, distinguishing the repeater type of each FRB. For this last case, we obtained <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:msub> <mml:mrow> <mml:mi>H</mml:mi> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mn>0</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msub> <mml:mo>=</mml:mo> <mml:mn>71.3</mml:mn> <mml:msubsup> <mml:mrow> <mml:mn>6</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mo>-</mml:mo> <mml:mn>3.94</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mo>+</mml:mo> <mml:mn>3.59</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msubsup> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> km <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:msup> <mml:mrow> <mml:mtext>s</mml:mtext> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mo>-</mml:mo> <mml:mn>1</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msup> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:msup> <mml:mrow> <mml:mtext>Mpc</mml:mtext> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mo>-</mml:mo> <mml:mn>1</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msup> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula>. Two models of the Milky Way were considered, and the YMW16 model consistently yielded higher <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:msub> <mml:mrow> <mml:mi>H</mml:mi> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mn>0</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msub> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> values than NE2001 model, ranging from <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:mo>+</mml:mo> <mml:mn>1.12</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> to <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:mo>+</mml:mo> <mml:mn>13.24</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> km <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:msup> <mml:mrow> <mml:mtext>s</mml:mtext> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mo>-</mml:mo> <mml:mn>1</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msup> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:msup> <mml:mrow> <mml:mtext>Mpc</mml:mtext> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mo>-</mml:mo> <mml:mn>1</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msup> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> across different host galaxy assumptions. The <inline-formula> <mml:math> <mml:mrow> <mml:msub> <mml:mrow> <mml:mi>H</mml:mi> </mml:mrow> <mml:mrow> <mml:mn>0</mml:mn> </mml:mrow> </mml:msub> </mml:mrow> </mml:math> </inline-formula> values obtained for each repeater type are implausible, suggesting that further modeling may be needed for these subpopulations. Finally, we used a catalog of 500 synthetic FRBs that allowed us to generate a deviation of 2.6% from the value obtained with the observed data, but allowed us to decrease the statistical precision from 5.5% to 1.2%, thus demonstrating the great potential that FRBs have as an alternative method to alleviate the Hubble Tension.

</details>


### [4] [The sound of dark sectors in pulsar timing arrays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.16282)
*Banik, Amitayus,Cui, Yanou,Tsai, Yu-Dai,Tsai, Yuhsin*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 该论文提出暗部中的相变可解释脉冲星计时阵列（PTAs）探测到的随机引力波信号，并在微观拉格朗日层面上系统刻画简单的暗部基础模型，其暗部特征标度为O(MeV)。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 脉冲星计时阵列（PTAs）最近探测到随机引力波（GW）信号。该文旨在从理论层面提出解释：暗部（DS）中的相变被认为是一种有前景的成因，而非仅用唯象参数拟合数据。

Method: 摘要未说明观测数据细节。作者在微观拉格朗日层面上系统地刻画简单的基础暗部（DS）模型，并推导产生PTAs所观测引力波谱所需的条件。

Result: 该文揭示了产生PTAs观测到的GW谱所需的暗部模型条件，并在此框架内得到暗部特征标度为O(MeV)。该模型可在未来的能量前沿和强度前沿实验中进行互补检验。

Conclusion: 作者提出暗部相变可解释PTAs探测到的随机引力波信号，表明暗部特征标度约为O(MeV)。根据摘要无法判断该结论与脉冲星计时或引力波观测方法本身的直接关联；其论证属于理论建模层面。

Abstract: A phase transition in the dark sector (DS) presents a promising explanation for the stochastic gravitational wave (GW) signals detected in recent observations by Pulsar Timing Arrays (PTAs). Instead of focusing solely on fitting data with phenomenological parameters, we systematically delineate simple, underlying DS models at the microscopic, Lagrangian level and uncover the conditions required to yield the GW spectrum observed by PTAs. Within this framework, the characteristic scale of the dark sector is <mml:math><mml:mi>O</mml:mi></mml:math>(MeV), with complementary tests at future energy- and intensity-frontier experiments.

</details>


### [5] [An X-ray census of fast radio burst sources]()
*Ashkar, Halim,Guessoum, Nidhal,Azeez, Zeeshan Ayyoor Abdul,Pattani, Nuha Manal,Hilles, Malak*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 本文对全部已被Swift观测的已知重复暴和定位良好的FRB进行了系统的X射线搜寻与分析。结果表明FRB祖源倾向于处于中等或较低X射线活动环境中，与中子星或磁星等致密天体一致，并给出迄今最大同质的FRB源持续X射线光度约束集。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: FRB的起源问题仍存在许多基本未解之处，尤其是其宿主星系和局部环境的性质。该研究旨在通过对FRB位置的系统性X射线分析，约束可能的FRB祖源候选体及其所在的X射线活动环境，填补对FRB持续与瞬变X射线发射缺乏同质系统约束的知识空白。

Method: 摘要未说明具体观测频率、时间跨度或样本规模。研究汇总所有已知的、具有公开数据的重复暴和定位良好的FRB，这些源已被Swift天文台观测；随后使用HEASARC工具对这些FRB位置进行系统的X射线分析，搜寻瞬变与持续辐射，并据此给出持续X射线光度约束。

Result: 摘要未给出具体数值、不确定度或统计显著性。研究搜寻结果不利于将最亮的X射线源作为主要的FRB祖源候选体，而倾向于处于中等或较低X射线活动环境中的FRB祖源，这与中子星或磁星等致密天体一致；并呈现了迄今最大同质的FRB源持续X射线光度约束集合。

Conclusion: 摘要所述结论支持FRB祖源更可能处于中等或较低X射线活动环境，符合中子星或磁星等致密天体的图像。作者提出，借助Chandra等更高灵敏度仪器及未来如NewAthena等天文台有望获得显著更深的约束，以探测暗弱X射线对应体与类ULX环境。此展望为作者表述的潜在方向，并非本文已确立的结果。

Abstract: Fast radio bursts (FRBs) are bright flashes of radio emission with millisecond durations. Many fundamental questions remain regarding their origin, in particular the nature of their host galaxies and local environments. In this study, we gather all known repeating and well-localized FRBs with publicly available data that have been observed by the Swift observatory. We perform a systematic X-ray analysis using HEASARC tools to search for both transient and persistent emission from the FRB positions. Our search disfavors the brightest X-ray sources as dominant FRB progenitor candidates and favors FRB progenitors in moderate or low X-ray activity environments, consistent with compact objects such as neutron stars or magnetars. We summarize our results and present the largest homogeneous set of constraints to date on the persistent X-ray luminosities of FRB sources. We discuss prospects for significantly deeper constraints with higher-sensitivity instruments such as Chandra and future observatories including NewAthena, enabling the exploration of faint X-ray counterparts and ULX-like environments.

</details>


### [6] [Modeling the Gravitational Wave Signal from the X-ray Pulsar Her X-1]()
*Markozov, I. D.,Pimenov, G. K.,Mushtukov, A. A.*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 本文将Her X-1的X射线脉冲星中子星视为自由进动椭球体，在四极近似下计算其引力波信号，构建$h_{\times}$和$h_{+}$偏振轮廓，评估DECIGO望远镜的探测可能性，并推导了以小椭率参数微扰法的半解析建模公式。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 本文旨在对X射线脉冲星Her X-1的引力波信号进行建模。科学背景是，如果将中子星视为自由进动的椭球体，其引力波信号可以在四极近似下计算。研究探索利用近期测得的Her X-1中子星进动参数来构建引力波信号，并评估其被DECIGO望远镜探测的可能性。

Abstract: We present deep Chandra observations of the pulsar wind nebula (PWN) powered by PSR J0007+7303 in the composite supernova remnant CTA 1. The merged ACIS image shows a ~20″ jet extending south of the pulsar and bending toward the southwest, a faint counterjet to the north, and a compact torus oriented approximately perpendicular to the jet axis. Using an archival observation from 2003, we perform relative astrometry over a ~20 yr baseline and constrain the pulsar's transverse velocity to ≲200 km s<SUP>-1</SUP> at a distance of 1.4 kpc at 95% confidence. This relatively low transverse velocity, along with the morphology of the jet, indicates a possible interaction with the SN reverse shock. Spatially resolved spectroscopy shows hard spectra for the jet and torus (photon indices Γ ≈ 1.2--1.4) and a softer spectrum for the extended nebula (Γ = 1.85 ± 0.11), indicating minimal radiative cooling in the compact regions. Modeling of the torus, associated with the termination shock, as an inclined circle yields a viewing angle ζ ≈ 50°. The outer gap and two-pole caustic pulsar emission models then imply a moderate magnetic inclination (α ~ 20°--70°). Broadband spectral energy distribution (SED) modeling from radio to PeV gamma rays for a one-zone leptonic scenario yields a low magnetic field (B ≈ 1.4--3.2 μG) and a high electron cutoff energy (E<SUB>cut</SUB> ~ 0.2--0.3 PeV), indicating that the magnetic field decreases rapidly outside of the compact nebula. These results establish CTA 1 as a young, low X-ray efficiency PWN with a hard injection spectrum capable of accelerating particles to PeV energies.

Method: 作者开展核反应网络模拟（nuclear reaction network simulations），研究磁星巨耀发的r-process核合成产额，并计算源于放射性核衰变的核伽马射线谱线发射。分析包括核素丰度分布、伽马射线不透明度的能量依赖性，以及不同时间与能段的伽马射线谱线通量特征。

Result: 磁星巨耀发合成的核素主要分布在第一和第二r-process丰度峰附近；因核素组成，伽马射线不透明度强烈依赖于能量，keV波段比MeV波段高约三个数量级。核伽马射线发射在早期（t ≲ 10^3 s）由MeV光子主导，随时间向sub-MeV和keV扩展。谱在~1 MeV附近出现峰值，主要贡献来自^88Kr和^92Sr，其放射性衰变产生数条通量超过~10^-8 erg cm^-2 s^-1的明亮谱线。

Conclusion: 作者认为磁星巨耀发提供满足r-process核合成的高熵快速膨胀条件，SGR 1806-20的晚期伽马射线辐射支持这一情景。由于银河系内磁星巨耀发速率比中子星并合高约三个数量级，其核伽马射线谱线可为当前MeV仪器所探测，为MeV伽马射线天文学带来新的科学机遇。

Abstract: The rapid neutron-capture process (r-process) is widely regarded as the dominant mechanism responsible for the synthesis of heavy elements in the universe, yet its astrophysical sites remain an open question. Recent studies suggest that the high-entropy, rapidly expanding baryonic material ejected by magnetar giant flares may provide favorable conditions for r-process nucleosynthesis, while the late-time gamma-ray emission observed from the magnetar SGR 1806-20 offers direct observational support for this scenario. In this work, we perform nuclear reaction network simulations to investigate the nucleosynthesis yields of magnetar giant flares and to characterize the associated nuclear gamma-ray line emission arising from the radioactive decay of heavy nuclei. The nuclei synthesized in magnetar giant flares are found to be mainly distributed near the first and second r-process abundance peaks. Owing to this nuclide composition, the gamma-ray opacity is found to be strongly energy-dependent, with the opacity in the keV band exceeding that in the MeV band by approximately three orders of magnitude. The nuclear gamma-ray emission is dominated by MeV photons at early times (t ≲ 10<SUP>3</SUP> s) and gradually extends toward the sub-MeV and keV bands as time progresses, thereby offering a diagnostic of heavy element enrichment in the ejecta. The gamma-ray spectrum exhibits a peak near ~ 1 MeV with major contributions from <SUP>88</SUP>Kr and <SUP>92</SUP>Sr, whose radioactive decays produce several bright gamma-ray lines with fluxes exceeding ~ 10<SUP>-8</SUP> erg cm<SUP>-2</SUP> s<SUP>-1</SUP>, making them the most promising lines for detection by MeV gamma-ray detectors. Because magnetar giant flares occur in the Galaxy at a rate roughly three orders of magnitude higher than neutron star mergers and their nuclear gamma-ray lines are accessible to current MeV instruments, they offer new and valuable science opportunities for MeV gamma-ray astronomy.

Abstract: We present analyses of new and archival Chandra and NuSTAR data on two old millisecond pulsars, a young highly-energetic pulsar, and a young neutron star. Its faintness suggests a distance more in accord with the further (≍1kpc) of its dispersion measure-inferred distances. PSR J1933-6211 is a 3.54 ms radio pulsar also with a carbon-oxygen white dwarf companion. Our first-time X-ray detection in an archival HRC-S image indicates its X-ray luminosity is similar to that of the 1.9 ⊙ pulsar PSR J1614-2230. Next, PSR J1813-1749 is a 1-2 kyr pulsar with one of the highest known spin-down luminosities. We measure its spin period of 44.7 ms using 2025 NuSTAR and 2026 Chandra ACIS-S data and update its spin evolution model. We show for the first time the pulse profile of PSR J1813-1749 at 3-79 keV, and we find that the spectra of the pulsar and pulsar wind nebula at these energies did not change significantly between observations in 2018 and 2025. Finally, for the 5-10 kyr neutron star CXOU J182913.1-125113 in the supernova remnant G18.95-1.1, we combine ACIS-I observations from 2009 and 2024 to better measure the neutron star's spectrum.

Abstract: Context. PSR J1913+1102 is a highly asymmetric double neutron star (DNS) system, making it an excellent laboratory for testing scalar-tensor gravity theories and a potential progenitor analogue of GW170817 that will merge in 470 Myr. Aims. To maximise the utility of this system for testing gravity theories and to better understand its origin, we carried out timing measurements with the aim of refining the proper motion and post-Keplerian (PK) parameters. Methods. We performed an updated timing analysis of the system by combining 13 years of data, consisting of historical Arecibo observations and the latest FAST measurements. We applied two different approaches to model the dispersion measure (DM) variations. Results. The new timing analysis enables precise measurements of four PK parameters, leading to improved precision in mass estimates for the system. Assuming general relativity (GR) and modelling the DM variation with a Gaussian process, we obtained a three-fold improvement in the total mass, m<SUB>tot</SUB> = 2.88948(20) M<SUB>⊙</SUB>, and a nearly four-fold improvement in the pulsar mass m<SUB>p</SUB> = 1.599(8) M<SUB>⊙</SUB>, the companion mass, m<SUB>c</SUB> = 1.290(8) M<SUB>⊙</SUB>, and, thus, in the mass ratio, q = 0.807(8) as well. This value is five times more precise than the previously published value and is fully consistent with the GR prediction for gravitational-wave damping. Abstract: The main types of energy losses experienced by ultrarelativistic electron-positron pulsar plasma in the upper atmosphere of a companion star are considered. These energy-loss mechanisms include ionization losses due to interactions with atoms of the medium, excitation losses associated with the energy transferred to plasma particles, losses resulting from the production of δ electrons, Cherenkov radiation losses, bremsstrahlung losses, synchrotron radiation losses, and inverse Compton losses. It is shown that the fastest component of the pulsar plasma can reach the top of the convection zone of the nearby companion star. The kinetic energy range of the plasma particles reaching the upper boundary of the convection zone corresponds to the energy range of the giant dipole resonance (GDR). As a result of the interaction between the pulsar plasma and the upper atmosphere of the companion star, localized regions of the photosphere and of the photosphere-convection zone boundary at different depths undergo nonuniform (spot-like) heating. This interaction may also produce neutron-rich isotopes, which are subsequently transported into the upper layers of the photosphere.

Abstract: We discuss the mechanism for FRB generation as the impact of Small Bodies (SBs) such as asteroids and comets onto a neutron star (NS), which was recently proposed by Geng and Huang (2015) as applied to young NSs, taking into account thermal effects such as sublimation and distant cracking of SBs due to thermal stresses. In such a scenario, the remnants of a planetary system of asteroid-comet origin, after entering the periphery of the planetary system of the newborn NS, return to the star along trajectories close to parabolic in their motion along elongated orbits. In this case, there is no difference in the origin of the infalling objects; they can also be interstellar objects (ISO). We also take into account the fact that, according to Pastor-Marazuela et al. (2025), young neutron stars are very hot objects, and the region where their destruction begins can be located much further from the star than assumed in previous studies. Heating of SBs by the star's radiation causes cracks to appear within these bodies, which then disintegrate into smaller fragments. Taking into account thermal processes, namely the thermal destruction of SBs, leading to their destruction at large distances from the NS, allows us to conclude that the duration of flares in FRB increases even for relatively small objects, ranging in radius from 10 to 50 m. We do not analyze binarity, the matter accretion from the binary component, the infall of large bodies, stellar mergers, or other exotic processes that cause FRBs, nor do we analyze the influence of magnetic fields. Our conclusions are applicable to a specific range of infalling body sizes and energies of about 10e35 ─ 10e41 erg, according to Pham and Hopkins et al. (2024), and cannot be applied to super-energetic flares.

Abstract: Sub-millisecond time resolution is essential for unveiling relativistic compact objects and explosive phenomena like Fast Radio Burst (FRBs). While high-cadence observations are standard in radio and high-energy astronomy, progress in the visible band has been slower. However, fast photometers like SiFAP2 are now probing the optical sky at sub-ms scales with impactful results. The recent detection of unexpectedly bright optical pulsations from 3 millisecond pulsars (MSPs) has opened a new window into relativistic particle acceleration and its interaction with accretion disks and intrabinary winds. These discoveries revealed diverse emission mechanisms, with optical efficiencies significantly exceeding predictions from rotation-powered models. Key questions remain regarding the universality of MSP optical pulsations and the processes driving fast variability challenges that require high-time-resolution optical/IR observations. In this talk, we discuss the potential of future facilities for a systematic exploration of the fast-varying sky. We present a pipeline to identify and characterize transients on sub-ms timescales. This tool has already uncovered a large population of fast optical events, demonstrating its versatility in searching for counterparts of FRBs and magnetar bursts, as well as tracking satellites and space debris. A major future challenge will be discerning astrophysical transients from anthropogenic objects, a crucial step for next-generation time-domain surveys.

</details>

Abstract: The study of accretion columns of X-ray pulsars helps us characterize accretion physics in this extreme regime of strong gravity and strong magnetic fields, while also advancing our understanding of neutron stars themselves. Previous observations of the X-ray pulsar Hercules X-1 revealed the presence of a highly broadened Fe K fluorescence emission line, associated with Doppler motions exceeding 0.1c, suggesting its origin in the accretion column. We recently obtained a high-spectral resolution view of the Fe K energy band of Her X-1 thanks to a 200 ks observation with the XRISM observatory. In this talk I will present new results from the analysis of this high-quality spectral dataset. We confirm the presence of a broad, symmetric line at 6.5 keV with a typical width of 1 keV. Performing a pulse-resolved analysis, we find that the feature is strongly variable with Her X-1 pulse phase. This is consistent with the origin in the accretion column, where strong variability is expected due to the rotation of the magnetosphere and the various projection, Doppler and relativistic effects associated with high infall velocities in the vicinity of the neutron star. Additionally, the variability pattern evolves with the 35-day cycle of Hercules X-1, supporting a scenario according to which the neutron star and its accretion columns undergo free precession, in agreement with recent X-ray polarimetric results from the IXPE observatory. Finally, I will discuss future applications of the modeling of this broad line in X-ray pulsars with physical spectral models, to detect and track neutron star free precession and advance our understanding of neutron star interiors.

Abstract: Detection of the cyclotron resonance scattering features (CRSFs or cyclotron lines) in the X-ray spectrum of a neutron star is the most reliable method for measuring its magnetic field. Moreover, it is known that in bright X-ray pulsars positions of cyclotron lines are strongly dependent on an instantaneous accretion rate, making them a valuable diagnostic tool sensitive to the geometry of the X-ray emission region. The situation with CRSFs is quite complicated for a well-known 4U 0115+63 - 3.6s X-ray pulsar in binary system with a Be-star. Despite numerous efforts there is still no unambiguous identification of a fundamental cyclotron absorption line, with number of lines and their positions varying in different works. In our research we trace the evolution of the 22 keV CRSF with accretion luminosity during two giant outbursts using the NuSTAR data. We compare CRSF positions between separate observations, obtained in different parts of outbursts. Also, thanks to the strong mHz quasiperiodic oscillations that are present at the outbursts peak, we are able to probe CRSF centroid energy variability on short timescales, using pulse-intensity resolved spectroscopy. Our analysis shows that, despite source being in supercritical accretion regime, cyclotron line centroid shifts to the higher energies as the luminosity increases, which comes to disagreement with predictions of the current theory of accretion onto magnetized neutron stars. We have also verified that such behavior holds for different continuum models, which makes our method more robust than the usual approach using a tentative fundamental line.

Abstract: More than 50 years after the discovery of neutron stars, their interior composition and structure remain unknown. Because the extreme densities and matter asymmetry in neutron star interiors are out of reach for Earth laboratories, the equation of state of bulk nuclear matter is unknown, with important implications for astrophysics and nuclear physics. Thankfully, measurements of neutron star masses and radii are direct probes of the interior of these compact objects. In the past two decades, X-ray observatories such as Chandra, XMM-Newton, and more recently NICER, have provided some measurements of neutron star radii and therefore some constraints on the dense matter equation of state. Each telescope has its unique capabilities to apply a specific method to measure masses and radii from observations of neutron stars. For example, Chandra has the spatial resolution to hunt quiescent low-mass X-ray binaries in the crowded cores of globular clusters to perform spectral analyses at very low-background levels, and uncontaminated by nearby sources. NICER, on the other hand, has a single-pixel 6 arcmin field of view with a fairly high background, but it relies on unprecedented timing resolution to perform pulse profile modelling of pulsars with millisecond spin periods. NewAthena will have sufficient spatial and timing resolution combined to a high collecting area and a low background to apply these various methods to measure masses and radii from observations of neutron stars. In this talk, I will present the future performances of NewAthena to constrain the EoS of dense matter with spectroscopy of quiescent low-mass X-ray binaries or with pulse profile modelling of millisecond pulsars. Preliminary simulations indicate that NewAthena will be able to reduce the uncertainties on the radius from about +/- 1 or 2 km currently to uncertainties of about +/- 200-300 m in a shorter exposure time than needed with current observatories.

</details>

Abstract: Discoveries of accreting millisecond X-ray pulsars (AMXPs) and transitional millisecond pulsars (tMSPs) have provided observational support for the idea that millisecond pulsars are spun up to millisecond periods by the accretion torques in low-mass X-ray binaries (LMXBs). In this study, we have employed the stellar evolution code MESA to investigate both the binary evolution and the accompanying neutron star evolution of LMXBs that form AMXPs by following three distinct evolutionary tracks. These tracks are defined according to the donor type in the resulting AMXP systems. We adopted a recently developed analytical model for the inner disc radius and the associated torque calculations across different rotational phases of LMXBs to explore the long-term rotational evolution of AMXPs. In this model, the inner disc radius depends on the spin period, the magnetic dipole moment, and the mass inflow rate of the disc. While the morphology of mass-transfer history depends on the magnetic braking index, we find that the initial orbital period is the most influential parameter shaping the binary evolution. For reasonable model parameters, we obtain spin periods and period derivatives consistent with the observed properties of AMXPs along these evolutionary tracks. For selected individual sources along each evolutionary track, we have shown that the model can simultaneously reproduce the NS and binary properties of AMXPs. Finally, we discuss the characteristic timescales and lifetimes of the evolutionary tracks, as well as the conditions under which these systems may be observable at different stages of their long-term evolution.

Abstract: In this talk, I will show our recent statistical study of broad-lined Type Ic supernovae (SNe Ic-BL), which includes 69 GRB-unassociated and 11 GRB-associated events. Using a magnetar central engine to model the early-time peak emission and ^56Ni decay for the late-time tail, our model successfully reproduces the multi-band light curves of these SNe. We find that the initial spin period of magnetars in SNe Ic-BL may range from ∼1 to 5ms, with magnetic field strengths between ∼10^15 and 10^16G. Importantly, we did not observe any statistically significant differences between GRB-associated and GRB-unassociated SNe Ic-BL. By fitting the inferred explosion energies and ejecta masses of SNe Ic-BL and comparing them with other superluminous SNe Ic (SLSNe Ic) and fast blue optical transients (FBOTs), we identified a universal relationship, suggesting a common origin. We confirmed this universal relationship using binary star evolution models, supporting the idea that SNe Ic-BL, SLSNe, and FBOTs may originate from a tidal scenario. Additionally, we found that SNe Ic-BL share similar ejecta and ^56Ni production properties with normal SNe Ic, implying that their main difference likely lies in whether their progenitors retain high rotation rates, enabling the formation of rapidly spinning magnetars that influence the explosion energetics. Based on these findings, we propose a unified model for these stripped-envelope supernovae.

The validated framework was then embedded directly into NBODY6++GPU, and test integrations spanning timescales from under a Megayear up to a Gigayear show that the resulting period-period derivative distribution already resembles the pattern seen in real globular cluster pulsar populations. Finally, an early production test followed ten neutron stars self-consistently through the integrated code over roughly 28 Myr, uncovering that most of them pass through at least one episode of backward evolution, a behaviour that only becomes visible when spin parameters are sampled at every simulation step rather than inferred afterwards. Taken together, these results show that pulsar physics can now evolve as an integral part of cluster dynamics rather than a separate bookkeeping exercise, setting the stage for a full production run at N = 250,000.

Concentrating on four different spinning configurations, we find that aligned, antialigned, and mixed (aligned/antialigned) spin configurations lead to markedly different growth rates of the electromagnetic (EM) energy, field topologies, and vortex properties when compared to the irrotational case. Abstract: Transitional millisecond pulsars (tMSPs) are a unique class of neutron star binaries that alternate between an accretion-powered low-mass X-ray binary (LMXB) state and a rotation-powered radio millisecond pulsar state, providing a direct link between accretion and pulsar recycling. The prototypical system PSR J1023+0038 is currently in an LMXB state and exhibits rapid switching between distinct high, low, and flaring modes across X-ray and optical wavelengths. Understanding the physical origin of these mode transitions is essential for constraining accretion processes operating at low luminosities. We present a comprehensive multiwavelength study of J1023 based on Kepler K2, XMM-Newton (optical monitor and X-ray), ULTRACAM, and MUSCAT observations spanning multiple epochs. We develop a dedicated analysis framework for optical light curves that removes orbital modulation and reveals intrinsic mode-dependent variability. Using statistical analyses of flux distributions, variability properties, and mode durations, we investigate correlations between emission states across different wavelengths. Particular attention is given to high─low mode transitions, their recurrence timescales, and the anti-correlation between X-ray and optical variability. Our results characterize mode-dependent emission properties and provide new constraints on the physical mechanisms driving rapid state switching in tMSPs. We further compare optical orbital modulation before and after the state transition of J1023, showing that optical emission in the radio MSP state is dominated by the irradiated companion star, while the LMXB state requires an additional accretion-disk component that modifies the orbital variability beyond a simple flux offset. These findings offer new insights into disk─magnetosphere interactions and accretion dynamics in transitional systems, contributing to a broader understanding of neutron-star LMXBs at the boundary between accretion- and rotation-powered regimes.

While the RVM can be fit in many cases to the polarization position angle dependence on phase, the prediction of the polarization degree with pulse phase is not as well modeled specifically with either "pencil" or "fan" beam models. Abstract: With spin periods shorter than 10 ms, millisecond pulsars are the fastest-spinning pulsars known. Often found in binary systems, millisecond pulsars achieve their high spin frequencies through a Gyr-long recycling phase involving the accretion of matter and angular momentum from a low-mass companion star. This phase ends when mass transfer declines, transforming the pulsar into a radio millisecond pulsar powered by the rotation of its magnetic field. The discovery of the accreting millisecond pulsar SAX J1808.4-3658 confirmed the recycling scenario, showing that mass accretion in a low-mass X-ray binary can spin up a neutron star to millisecond periods. SAX J1808.4-3658 was also the first of this class to show optical/UV pulsations during a bright accretion event. Challenging conventional rotation or accretion-powered scenarios, these pulsations offer key insights into the different emission mechanisms of pulsating neutron stars. I will present optical observations performed with the fast optical photometer TNG/SiFAP2 during the 2022 outburst of SAX J1808, aimed at investigating the nature of the pulsations at these energies. Optical pulsations detected during the 2022 outburst are compared with those observed for the first time in 2019, allowing us to study their evolution across different outbursts. We find an anti-correlation between the X-ray flux and the amplitude of the optical pulsations, with the latter increasing as the X-ray flux decreases. I will also discuss optical observations obtained during the quiescent state of the source, probing the pulse emission mechanism over a large range of mass accretion rates.

Abstract: Fast Radio Bursts (FRBs) are among the most enigmatic transients in the time-domain sky, and their interpretation increasingly relies on coordinated multiwavelength and multimessenger observations. These findings favour magnetars as the primary progenitors, while leaving open alternative channels such as hyperaccreting X-ray binaries or compact-object mergers in specific environments. In the next decade, new wide-field radio facilities (e.g. SKA, DSA-2000, ngVLA precursors) are expected to deliver dozens of arcsecond-localized FRBs per year, dramatically improving host-galaxy statistics and enabling systematic searches for persistent continuum counterparts. The scientific return will depend critically on rapid alert dissemination, coordinated follow-up across radio, optical, X-ray and gamma-ray facilities, and efficient data exploitation frameworks within the multimessenger ecosystem. This contribution reviews the current observational status of persistent radio emission associated with FRBs and discusses future prospects in the context of multimessenger synergies, highlighting the role of next-generation radio observatories in bridging transient discovery, host characterization, and cross-facility coordination.

Abstract: Low-mass X-ray binaries (LMXBs) are the best sources to study the interaction of the neutron stars (NSs) with the geometrically thin optically thick accretion disks. Significant developments in the theory of accretion disks helped us understand the physical mechanisms that can account for most of their observed characteristics. While it is commonly accepted that millisecond pulsars are spun up to millisecond periods by the accretion torques in these systems, as supported by the discoveries of accreting millisecond pulsars (AMXPs) and the transitional millisecond pulsars, there are still long-lasting problems of LMXBs which cannot be explained by the conventional models: (1) ongoing accretion at low X-ray luminosities, L_x, and transitions to the propeller phase at L_x levels much below those required for the spin-up/spin-down transitions, (2) torque reversals without significant changes in L_x and torque magnitudes on the two sides of the torque reversals, and (3) the lack of X-ray pulsations from most of LMXBs. We have developed an analytical model to calculate the inner disk radius, r_in, the torques acting on the neutron star (NS) in different rotational phases, and the critical conditions for the transitions between these phases in LMXBs. We have shown that the model can account for the three problems listed above. The difference between the predictions of our model and the conventional models is basically due to the difference in the r_in calculation. Conventionally, r_in is estimated to be close to Alfven radius, r_A. In our model, r_in depends on spin period as well as mass inflow rate of the disk and the magnetic dipole moment of the NS, and is found to be much smaller than r_A in both the strong propeller and the spin-up phases. We summarize the results of our model calculations and applications.

The <inline-formula><tex-math>${}^{3}P_2$</tex-math></inline-formula> superfluidity plays an important role in cooling curves with not the 2SC but 2SC+<inline-formula><tex-math>$\mathinner {\langle {dd}\rangle }$</tex-math></inline-formula> phases due to the suppression of quark <inline-formula><tex-math>$\beta$</tex-math></inline-formula>-decay. We therefore point out that, if the scenario of the 2SC+<inline-formula><tex-math>$\mathinner {\langle {dd}\rangle }$</tex-math></inline-formula> phase is true, it could be specified through low-temperature observations such as those of Vela, 3C58, Vela Jr., and Vela-like pulsar.

Abstract: Most X-ray pulsars in high-mass X-ray binary (HMXB) systems exhibit both global and local trends of spin acceleration (spin-up) and deceleration (spin-down). Moreover, decades-long monitored objects display even more general spin-up trends in their spin evolution and simultaneously demonstrate a decrease in the orbital period, so-called orbital decay. Although obvious, those general behavior leaves the open question on the energy source and the mechanism of angular momentum transfer that ensures the neutron star's spin acceleration. We hypothesize that the observed spin acceleration of X-ray pulsars in HMXB (RLO and sgXRB) systems results from orbital angular momentum transfer via tidal interactions with their massive companions. To study these phenomena, we sample five well-studied objects with the longest observational history of their spin periods from the population of known Galactic persistent X-ray pulsars in HMXB systems. We obtain the spin frequency change from the observational data and estimate energy loses for orbital decay and pulsars's spin acceleration. We propose a new term of super-global spin-up trends, and show that their observable values do not exceed theoretical upper limits predicted within the scenario of orbital decay due to the tidal interaction between the neutron star and its massive companion [Kim V. and Aimuratov Y., 2025, ApJ, Vol. 991, id.42].

Abstract: Transitional millisecond pulsars (tMSPs) bridge the gap between accreting neutron stars in low-mass X-ray binaries and millisecond radio pulsars, offering a unique laboratory to study the interplay between accretion and pulsar activity. These systems exhibit a subluminous X-ray state characterized by alternating high, low and flaring emission modes. Multi-wavelength campaigns on the prototype tMSP, PSR J1023+0038, have helped establish a solid understanding of how tMSPs are powered. A complex interplay between a compact jet, discrete ejecta, accreting matter and the pulsar wind seem to be regulating the behaviour of this puzzling source. Recently, using polarimetric data from the Imaging X-ray Polarimetry Explorer (IXPE), the Very Large Telescope (VLT), and the Karl G. Jansky Very Large Array (VLA), we conducted the first multiwavelength polarimetric study of PSR J1023+0038. A linear polarization of (12±3)% in the 2─6 keV band was observed during the high mode. The polarization angle aligns with the optical polarization observed by the VLT, suggesting a shared physical mechanism. During the low mode, the significance was insufficient for detailed analysis, resulting in an upper limit of 26% (90% confidence) on the polarization degree. The results strongly indicate that both optical and X-ray polarization originate from synchrotron radiation at the boundary between the pulsar wind and the inner accretion disc. Finally, simultaneous radio, optical, and X-ray observations obtained as part of this campaign have shed light on the poorly understood flaring mode emission, emphasizing the critical role of outflows in tMSPs.

</details>

Abstract: Millisecond pulsars are spun up during their accretion phase in a binary system. The exchange of angular momentum between the accretion disk and the star tends to align the spin and orbital angular momenta on a very short time scale compared to the accretion stage. This alignment can also be seen as the equality between two angles : the orbital inclination angle i, corresponding to the angle between the line-of-sight of an observer and the orbital angular momentum ; and the viewing angle ζ, corresponding to the angle between the line-of-sight and the rotation axis. In this work, we studied a subset of gamma-ray millisecond pulsars in binaries for which the orbital inclination angle i has been accurately constrained thanks to the Shapiro delay measurements. Our goal is to constrain the observer viewing angle ζ and to check whether it agrees with the orbital inclination angle i, in other words if ζ is approximately equal to i. We use a Bayesian inference technique to fit the millisecond pulsars gamma-ray light curves based on the third gamma-ray pulsar catalogue (3PC). The emission model relies on the striped wind model deduced from force-free neutron star magnetosphere simulations. We found good agreement between the two angles i and ζ for a significant fraction of our sample, about four fifth, confirming the spin-orbit alignment scenario during the accretion stage. However about one fifth of our sample deviates significantly from this alignment. The reasons are manifold: either the gamma-ray fit is not reliable or some precession and external torque avoid an almost perfect alignment.

</details>

Abstract: Context. Fast radio bursts often exhibit strong linear polarization together with pronounced spectral structure. Yet in coherent curvature radiation, spectral decoherence and depolarization are not necessarily simultaneous. Aims. Abstract: Pulsar Timing Array experiments are rapidly approaching the era of gravitational wave background anisotropy detection. The timing residuals of each pulsar are an integrated measure of the gravitational-wave power across all angular scales. However, due to the limited number of monitored pulsars, current analyses are only able to reconstruct the angular structure of the background at large scales. We show analytically that this mismatch between the integrated all-sky signal and the truncated reconstruction introduces a previously unaccounted source of systematic bias in the anisotropic background angular power spectrum. The source of this systematic error, that we call ``small-scale leakage'', is the intrinsic presence of unaccounted gravitational wave power at scales smaller than the reconstructed scales. This unmodeled power leaks into large-scale modes, artificially increasing the recovered value of the inferred angular power spectrum by at least one order of magnitude in a wide range of scales. Importantly, this effect is fundamentally independent of the geometry of the pulsar configuration, the anisotropy reconstruction method, the use of different regularization schemes, and the presence of pulsar noise. As the quality of pulsar timing array experiments improves, a robust understanding of small-scale leakage will become paramount for reliable detection and characterization of the gravitational wave background. Thus, the theoretical formalism developed here will be essential to estimate the magnitude of this systematic uncertainty in anisotropy searches.

Abstract: Accreting millisecond X-ray pulsars (AMXPs) are transient low-mass X-ray binaries (LMXBs) in which neutron stars (NSs) are spun up via mass transfer from their low-mass companions. AMXPs are thought to be evolving in the final evolutionary phases of LMXBs. These sources provide a rich laboratory to study the disk-field interaction and the resultant spin evolution of NSs. In this work, we have used an analytical model developed to explain some typical torque-luminosity behaviors and torque reversals of LMXBs to investigate the torques acting on AMXPs during their outburst and quiescent phases. We have compared the model predictions with the observations of five AMXPs with known rotational properties and X-ray luminosities. Our results obtained from the model calculations are consistent with the observed outburst and quiescent properties, yielding an average spin-down, which is also in aggrement with the observed long-term spin-down trend of these sources. To put in other words, the disk, accretion, and magnetic dipole torques are sufficient to account for the observed long-term spin-down of these AMXPs without invoking additional spin-down torques. Nevertheless, our results are not sufficient to rule out the spin-down contribution of the gravitational radiation torques due to significant timing noise and uncertainties about the bolometric corrections for X-ray luminosity during the outbursts of some sources.

Abstract: PSR J1023+0038 is a transitional millisecond pulsar (tMSP), the only one where coherent pulsations were discovered in both the Optical and X-ray energy bands to date. We present a detailed timing analysis of new Aqueye+ optical data taken from 2021 through 2023, obtaining an updated timing solution for the pulsar. We perform a new measurement of the phase lag between optical and X-ray pulsations and investigate the recent evolution of the binary system, monitoring the variation of the time of passage at the ascending node. We measured a phase lag between the optical and X-ray pulses of 112.3 ± 30.7 μs, confirming a common origin. After 2017, the evolution of the passage at the ascending node follows a well-defined parabolic increasing trend, suggesting a corresponding increment in the orbital period and orbital separation of the system. This evolution is consistent with a scenario in which the binary system is evolving through non-conservative Roche lobe overflow and the donor is interacting with the pulsar wind, losing mass at a rate much higher than the inferred accretion rate. Finally, we offer a glimpse of the results from observations of PSR J1023+0038 obtained with Iqueye recently installed at Gemini South, which achieved unprecedented photon statistics and time resolution.

Abstract: An increasing number of pulsar wind nebulae (PWNe) are being identified in the TeV band by ground-based Imaging Air Cherenkov Telescopes such that they constitute the dominant source class of Galactic TeV emitters. However, MeV-GeV PWN counterparts are still largely lacking. To date, only a dozen PWNe are identified by the Fermi─Large Area Telescope (LAT) in the MeV-GeV band. Most PWNe are located along the Galactic plane embedded within the prominent, diffuse Galactic γ-ray emission, which makes these sources difficult to disentangle from the bright diffuse background. We present a systematic search for γ-ray counterparts to known PWNe in the 300 MeV - 2 TeV energy band using the Fermi-LAT. We target the locations of previously identified PWNe that lack detected Fermi─LAT pulsars to minimize associated pulsar contamination. The sample includes six previously identified Fermi PWNe and eight Fermi─LAT sources associated with PWNe. We report the analysis of 58 regions of interest and classify Fermi─LAT detected sources as either a likely PWN or a candidate PWN counterpart based on their morphological and spectral characteristics across the broadband spectrum. There are nine unidentified Fermi─LAT sources that we consider as likely PWN counterparts, which, if confirmed to be PWNe, would greatly increase the PWN population detected by the Fermi─LAT from 12 to 21. The remaining Fermi─LAT detected sources are considered weaker PWN candidates. A second approach in the systematic search for γ-ray emitting PWNe will involve studying the off-pulse phases of Fermi─LAT detected pulsars for the presence of an obscured PWN and will be reported in a subsequent paper.

Abstract: The Imaging X-ray Polarimetry Explorer (IXPE), launched on December 9, 2021, has, within its first four years of operation, successfully measured X-ray polarization in the 2─8 keV energy band for at least six Pulsar Wind Nebulae (PWNe): the Crab, Vela, MSH 15─52, B0540─69, 3C 58, and G21.5─0.9. Leveraging its imaging polarimetric capabilities, IXPE has enabled the first spatially resolved maps of X-ray polarization in PWNe, providing new constraints on the global magnetic-field structure and the level of turbulence within these systems. Owing to their strong synchrotron emission, PWNe have emerged as one of the most highly polarized classes of sources observed by IXPE to date, with polarization degrees in some cases approaching the theoretical maximum for synchrotron radiation. This indicates particle acceleration occurring in highly ordered magnetic fields. By isolating the nebular contribution, significant phase-resolved polarization from the embedded pulsars has also been detected in several cases. Although there is a general trend toward aligned polarization in young PWNe exhibiting jet─torus X-ray morphologies, the full population displays a wide range of polarization properties, suggesting underlying physical differences that are not yet fully understood. In this contribution, I will review the main results from IXPE observations of PWNe, highlighting both common patterns and key differences, and discussing their implications for particle acceleration and magnetic-field structure in these systems.

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 瞬变源（如GRBs，以及一定程度上FRBs）凭借其大红移，是确定源形成率（FR）历史及其与恒星形成率（SFR）关系的良好候选者，从而为理解宇宙从黑暗时代到现今的演化提供关键信息。

Method: 回顾运用Lynden-Bell以及Efron和Petrosian（EP）非参数化方法对GRBs（若时间允许也包括FRBs）的光度函数（LF）与形成率演化进行估计。数据来源为Neil Gehrels Swift天文台，以及Konus-Wind和Fermi-GBM的GRB数据，FRB数据来自CHIME。样本需为测得流量与红移且截断确定的"完备样本"，并讨论用机器学习（ML）估计红移以缓解选择效应。

Abstract: Formation rate (FR) history of sources provide crucial information on how the universe emerged from the dark ages to its current stage. Best observed FR is that of stars (SFR) that rises from high redshifts to redshift of 3 and declines to today. Active galactic nuclei (AGNs) show a similar trend but with different parameters. Transient sources like gamma-ray bursts (GRBs), and to some degree fast radio bursts (FRBs), by virtue of their large redshifts are good candidates for determining this history and its relation to SFR. Methods: Achievement of this task requires determination of the "Luminosity Function" (LF) and the cosmological evolution of luminosity and FRs. This is often done by a forward fitting of the data to some assumed parametric forms for the LF and evolution, with many parameters, and often ignoring luminosity evolution. However, recent works use the non-parametric methods developed by Lynden-Bell and Efron and Petrosian (EP), which determines the luminosity evolution directly from "truncated" data. In this talk I will review past and more recent estimates of LFs and FR evolution, using theses methods, of GRBs and if time permits FRBs. Data: A critical issue here is to have samples with measured "fluxes" and redshifts (z) with well defined truncation determined by observational selection effects, which we call "complete samples". The main source for GRBs has been the Neil Gehrels Swift Observatory with some data from Konus-Wind and Fermi-GBM. The main source for FRBs has been the CHIME radio telescope. A main uncertainty is the selection effects that redshift measurements introduce. As I will discuss use of Machine Learning (ML) to estimates redshifts can help in this regard. Theory: An important issue for the transient sources, which are often poorly localized is their progenitors. GRBs are divided into two groups; Long-soft and Short-hard. So-called collapsars are assumed to be the progenitors of GRBs, while SGRBs progenitors are believed to be neutron star(NS)-NS or NS-black hole mergers, which are also sources of gravitational waves (GWs) and Kilonovae. FRBs also come in two variety "repeaters" and "non-repeaters" with magnetars as likely progenitors. Results: GRBs consistently shown (1) presence of strong luminosity evolution,a broken power-law LF and varied formation rate evolution. Collapsars, being short lived stars are expected to follow the SFR. We find that LGRBs seem to follow the SFR at high-z's but deviate significantly from it, as a low low-z component that increase with decreasing-z. SGRBs show a increasing FR up to lowest z's similar to delayed SFR expected from compact object mergers, similar to the low-z component of LGRB. These finding raise doubt about the standard separation by duration of GRBs and affects the expected frequency of GWs and Kilonovae. FRBs also show luminosity evolution and a delayed FR supporting the magnetar origin. This work is carried out with extensive collaboration with Maria Dainotti and several graduate and undergraduate students.

</details>


### [60] [Resurgence and hyperasymptotics in wave optics astronomy]()
*Feldbrugge, Job,Crew, Samuel,Pen, Ue-Li*

Abstract: With the discovery of gravitational waves and fast radio bursts, wave optics has become increasingly relevant in astrophysics. This paper studies the behaviour of random gravitational and plasma lenses, presenting the refractive and diffractive expansions, with higher-order terms that allow error estimates and embody the counterintuitive resurgence phenomenon. Specifically, we show that the diffractive expansion converges for a broad class of bounded lens models and provides an efficient description of interference patterns across frequency regimes. Next, building on Picard-Lefschetz techniques, we derive the full refractive expansion, organising it into a transseries. Near caustics, the standard transseries is supplemented with uniform asymptotics. We study this transseries, with both Borel and hyperasymptotic resummation yielding systematic approximations to lensing integrals at all frequencies. Our results give a framework for modelling wave optics lensing near caustics and beyond the geometric optics approximation and thereby illustrate how tools from resurgence and asymptotic analysis can be applied to practical problems in astrophysics. Near caustic singularities, the post-refractive corrections diverge, while the uniform asymptotic expansion becomes accurate. We use the leading uniform approximation to bridge the singular caustic regime. For the Lorenzian lens, the leading-order diffractive-refractive-uniform expansions mosaic the full parameter space, providing quantitative switching criteria.

Abstract: We use cyclic spectroscopy to infer the scintillation parameter C<SUB>1</SUB> for the millisecond pulsar B1937+21. At 428 MHz, we find a weighted mean and standard deviation across multiple epochs and independent scintillation fluctuations of C<SUB>1</SUB> = 1.21 ± 0.01, which, along with the consistent presence of diffuse scintillation arcs, favors a thick-screen geometry spanning just over 10% of the Earth--pulsar distance. The resulting precision in our weighted average allows us to rule out various thin-screen geometries, as well as thick-screen geometries comprising more than 30% of the Earth--pulsar distance, with greater than 5σ certainty at this observing frequency. We also use our measured C<SUB>1</SUB> values to determine diffraction scales, which we find to be roughly 11 × 10<SUP>3</SUP> km between 418 and 438 MHz, suggesting an inner scale on the order of 10<SUP>3</SUP> km. Future implementations of our method to other lines of sight through the Galaxy may substantially improve efforts to understand structures that contribute to the majority of pulsar emission scattering in the interstellar medium. As flagship instruments like the Green Bank Telescope begin offering the use of cyclic spectroscopy backends, and other instruments begin exploration and commissioning of similar systems, demonstrations like these will be crucial for the widespread adoption of cyclic spectroscopy.

Abstract: EP251023a is an extragalactic fast X-ray transient (eFXT) that was only detected by the Einstein Probe (EP) and that lacks a gamma-ray counterpart. The prompt emission consisted of a main emission with a duration T<SUB>90</SUB> = 292 ± 19 s, followed by a long-lasting tail emission that persisted until the observation ended at T<SUB>0</SUB> + 1571 s. We identified a redshift of z = 2.232 ± 0.001 based on the strong absorption features in the Keck spectrum, which also indicate a relatively low host-galaxy HI column density. Based on the broadband spectral energy distribution, the late-time light curves show an achromatic plateau, followed by an extremely steep decay with a slope of 3.99 after a break at about 49 ks. Under the isotropic wind scenario, we obtained an initial period P<SUB>0</SUB> &lt; 2.27 ms and a magnetic field strength B<SUB>p</SUB> &lt; 8.33 × 10<SUP>14</SUP> G for the magnetar. If a jet collimation with a typical opening angle of 0.1 rad is considered, these constraints are relaxed to P<SUB>0</SUB> &lt; 32.15 ms and B<SUB>p</SUB> &lt; 1.18 × 10<SUP>16</SUP> G. Abstract: XMM-Newton observations of NGC 5907 ULX-1 offer a unique opportunity to investigate the spectral evolution of a pulsating ultraluminous X-ray source over a long period with X-ray luminosities varying by up to one order of magnitude. NGC 5907 ULX-1 is the most luminous ultraluminous X-ray pulsar known to date and one of the ULX pulsars that exhibits spin-down during its low flux states. Benefiting from the long-term monitoring of the source between 2003-2024, we present the results of a systematic search for the best physically motivated model to fit the continuum of the X-ray spectrum with the help of different emission models and modeling procedures. Such an approach also serves to resolve degeneracies between thermal and non-thermal emission at low and high energies. As a natural outcome of our analysis, we study the correlation between the thermal and non-thermal model parameters such as the inner disk temperature, the photon index, and the electron temperature for Comptonization with respect to the spin observability and the X-ray luminosity of NGC 5907 ULX-1.

Abstract: Millisecond pulsars (MSPs) are the quickest-spinning neutron stars (NSs) known. The X-ray pulsations produced by hot spots on their surface encode precious information on the mass and size of these compact objects; in this frame, accreting MSPs (AMSPs) are among the best targets because during X-ray outbursts they become relatively bright X-ray sources. Yet, breaking the degeneracy between the many parameters shaping their pulse profiles was not possible because of the limited effective area of X-ray instruments flown so far. X-ray polarimetry is a potential game-changer that provides an independent measure of the geometry of the hot spots. IXPE observed the outburst of SRGA J144459.2-604207 that occurred in February 2024 during a 10-day-long campaign also involving NICER, XMM-Newton, and NuSTAR. This observation provided the first detection of polarization in X-rays for an AMSP. The polarized signal showed a significantly energy-dependent polarization, and, thanks to an accurate pulse-timing solution, a phase-resolved polarimetric analysis was performed. Although the spin-phase variability of the normalized Stokes parameters is not highly significant, applying the relativistic rotating vector model, the hot spots' geometry was determined. The result indicates a moderately fan-beam emission from two point-like hot spots at a small magnetic obliquity. IXPE also detected 52 type-I X-ray bursts, which allowed for obtaining an upper limit on the polarization degree. In 2025, SAX J1808.4-3658 went into outburst, and IXPE observed it; preliminary analyses show a significant polarization also for this AMSP and the presence of a very bright type I X-ray burst in the light In this talk, the results obtained for the only two AMSP observed by IXPE so far are reported.

Abstract: 4U 1626─67 is a benchmark system for understanding complex accretion physics in ultracompact low-mass X-ray binaries (LMXBs). A defining characteristic of this LMXB hosting an accreting neutron star is the presence of a stable ∼48 mHz Quasi-Periodic Oscillation (QPO), exhibited exclusively during the spin-down torque state of the pulsar. We present a comprehensive QPO-phase-resolved study of the source using archival XMM-Newton observations to probe its accretion dynamics. We employed Variational Mode Decomposition (VMD) to isolate the QPO signal and extract its instantaneous phase. By resolving the 0.5─10 keV pulse profile morphology over the QPO cycle, we observe a significant evolution in the beaming pattern. This timing analysis is integrated with phase-resolved spectroscopy using simultaneous EPIC-PN and RGS data. We find that the spectral parameters exhibit strong variability with the QPO phase, pointing towards a dynamic X-ray reprocessing scenario. We discuss these findings from the perspective of the neutron star beaming geometry and inner accretion disk structure in 4U 1626─67.

Motivation: 热核（I型）X射线暴可作为强辐射、强引力、强磁场环境下核聚变过程的研究平台，其中最强的暴（光球半径膨胀暴）会将光球推离恒星表面。尽管已探测到数千次暴发，此前从未观察到单次暴发中出现两次连续的光球半径膨胀事件，这一现象成为本文研究的问题与知识空白。

Result: 首次在单次暴发中观测到两次连续的光球半径膨胀事件。黑体归一化在暴发期间显示两次连续的高峰，并与黑体温度下降的时刻对齐。这种不寻常行为可能表示同一次暴中的两次连续光球半径膨胀事件，或中子星大气的系统性金属丰度演化；但光谱分析表明金属丰度演化这一解释对IGR J17591-2342不太可能发生。

Conclusion: 作者将观测到的行为解释为单次热核X射线暴中出现两次连续的光球半径膨胀事件，并认为针对IGR J17591-2342的系统性金属丰度演化解释不太可能成立；由于该现象为首次观测且与单纯成分演化预测不符，结果对当前理论理解提出挑战。根据摘要无法判断其与脉冲星模式转换或FRB现象有何关联。

Abstract: Intermittent unstable thermonuclear burning of accreted matter on neutron stars gives rise to thermonuclear (type-I) X-ray bursts. Such bursts provide a testbed to study nuclear fusion processes in intense radiation, gravity, and magnetic fields. The strongest ones among such bursts temporarily push the photosphere away from the stellar surface, and are known as photospheric radius-expansion bursts. Although several thousand bursts have been detected over the past decades, here we report the first observation of a single burst with two consecutive photospheric radius-expansion events. This burst was found from the AstroSat observation of the accretion-powered millisecond X-ray pulsar IGR J17591-2342. Our basic spectral model for the burst includes an absorbed blackbody to describe the burst emission and an absorbed power law to represent the non-burst emission. The blackbody normalisation shows two consecutive humps aligned with blackbody temperature dips during the burst. Such an unusual behaviour could imply two consecutive photospheric radius-expansion events during the same burst or a systematic metallicity evolution in the neutron star atmosphere. However, our spectral analysis suggests the latter option is less likely to be happening for IGR J17591-2342. This result challenges current theoretical understanding. [A A, 701, A54 (2025)].

</details>

Abstract: Binary stars are very important in Astronomy. In some cases, it is possible to access, with good precision, the fundamental parameter that determines the evolution of an isolated star: its mass. In close binary systems, if close enough to each other, the evolution of the components changes completely compared to what each of them would have had in isolation, because mass loss may occur by Roche lobe overflow. Examples of objects due to binary evolution are low-mass helium white dwarfs, type Ia supernova progenitors, cataclysmic variables, black widows and redbacks families, blue and yellow stragglers. In addition to the two stellar components, there is another fundamental actor: the accretion disk. Accretion disks have been studied for decades. However, the complexity of the physical processes that occur in it means that we still have a lot to explore. In this poster, we will make a brief description of the evolution of close binary systems composed of a low-mass normal star and a neutron star, using models obtained from our binary evolution code. We show the method we use to search for a possible progenitor of PSR J1622-0315, a redback whose characteristic parameters have been recently determined. Subsequently, we describe the thin disk treatment (``alpha disk''), where it is possible to decouple the vertical from the radial treatment (where temporal evolution is found). We present our first results and discuss the steps to follow to study the effect of neutron star irradiation on the accretion disk.

Abstract: A theory of the puzzling spectral "zebra" pattern of the Crab pulsar's high-frequency interpulse (HFIP) is developed. Thus, it solves a nearly 20-year-old mystery of the Crab pulsar radio emission. The observed emission bands are the high-contrast interference maxima, resulting from the combined effects of gravitational lensing and plasma de-lensing, and produced by multiple ray propagating through the pulsar magnetosphere. The model enables space-resolved tomography (like a CT scan) of the pulsar magnetosphere, yielding a plasma profile of n_e∝ 1/r^3, which agrees with theoretical insights. The model essentially resolves a 10-100-km scale at a distance of 2kpc — nearly million times better than EHT, which observed a galactic black hole. We predict the zebra pattern trend to change at a higher frequency when the ray separation becomes smaller than the pulsar size. This frequency is predicted to be in the range between 42 GHz and 650 GHz, which is within the reach of existing facilities like ALMA and SMA. These observations would advance our understanding of the magnetosphere and potentially explore gravity in the strong field regime near the star's surface. — The author acknowledges support by the National Science Foundation (NSF) under grant PHY-2409249. This research was supported in part by grant NSF PHY-2309135 to the Kavli Institute for Theoretical Physics (KITP).

</details>

Conclusion: 作者认为IXPE的结果已在多个方面挑战并拓展了对宇宙运行方式的理解，涵盖粒子加速、散射、强引力以及磁化等离子体中辐射输运等过程。摘要属于对未来探测路径的讨论。据此无法判断报告结论是否可直接或具体迁移至脉冲星、磁星、FRB等观测主题之外的定量研判；其涉及脉冲星风云与磁星的部分仅作为IXPE成果内容被陈述，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: At the beginning of X-ray astronomy, it was predicted that polarimetry would play a major diagnostic role in this field. Early polarimetry experiments followed the first advances of X-ray astronomy by only a few years. However, when Riccardo Giacconi revolutionized the field with focusing X-ray optics, polarimetry, whose sensitivity was far inferior, was set aside for many decades. The situation changed dramatically with the invention of detectors based on the photoelectric effect in gas. The Imaging X-ray Polarimetry Explorer, based on this technique, has extended to polarimetry the breakthrough in sensitivity achieved in imaging X-ray astronomy with the Einstein Observatory. Launched on 9 December 2021, IXPE has so far observed more than 100 sources across all major source classes and detected significant polarization in nearly 80% of them. In many cases, IXPE results have challenged several established views in relativistic astrophysics, expanding our understanding of how the Universe works. The mission has shed light on particle acceleration in pulsar wind nebulae, supernova remnants, and blazars; on scattering processes in molecular clouds around the Galactic Center, in black-hole binaries, radio-quiet active galactic nuclei, and in weakly magnetized neutron-star binaries; on strong-gravity effects also in black-hole binaries; and on the transport of radiation in highly magnetized plasma at the poles of neutron stars, as well as in the extreme magnetospheres of magnetars. In this talk I will review the main scientific results obtained so far with IXPE and discuss possible paths to extend these achievements through future X-ray polarimetry missions in upcoming opportunities.

</details>

Magnetar ellipticities are key parameters that could distinguish the role of these precessing compact objects from that of precessing disks. The lack of observations of gravitational waves from magnetars, such as pulsars, is another important piece of the puzzle. Inferences on magnetar parameters in the literature will be compared, and a coherent scenario will be presented at the conference.

Abstract: The Fermi Gamma-ray Burst Monitor (GBM) detects on average 240 gamma-ray bursts (GRBs) per year, providing the most comprehensive all-sky view of the transient gamma-ray sky. GBM detected the first—and so far only—short-GRB counterpart to a binary neutron star merger observed via gravitational waves (GWs), a discovery that connected relativistic jets, nucleosynthesis, and multi-messenger astrophysics, and helped define the science landscape for future missions. Following the completion of the fourth observing run (O4) of the LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA Collaboration (LVK), no additional joint GRB─GW detections have yet been confirmed. Throughout O4, the Fermi-GBM team conducted real-time searches for coincident gamma-ray emission from all public GW events using on-board triggers as well as two complementary sub-threshold pipelines: the Targeted and Untargeted Searches. These analyses allow GBM to place stringent upper limits on prompt gamma-ray emission and produce joint upper-limit skymaps. With its large field of view and continuous monitoring, GBM remains a cornerstone instrument in the era of data-intensive time-domain astronomy, capable of detecting counterparts—or setting meaningful limits—for a wide range of transient phenomena. I will present our follow-up efforts and highlight key GBM results, from fast radio bursts to long-duration gamma-ray counterparts from compact mergers.

</details>


### [72] [Relativistic Monte Carlo modeling of cyclotron lines in subcritical X-ray pulsars]()
*Fotiadis, Prodromos,Kylafis, Nikolaos,Loudas, Nick,Trümper, Joachim E.*

TL;DR: 本文提出一套完全相对论蒙特卡罗辐射转移代码，研究亚临界X射线脉冲星中回旋共振散射特征（CRSFs）的起源，并成功再现E_CRSF与σ_CRSF随光度及视角余弦的正/负相关关系，以及对XRP GX 304-1的观测验证。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 数值与解析计算虽能研究X射线脉冲星的吸积动力学，但难以转化为定量谱预言，因此需专门的辐射转移模拟来探测中子星表面附近的物理并解释观测数据，特别是探究回旋共振散射特征（CRSFs）的起源。

Method: 采用完全相对论的蒙特卡罗辐射转移代码；采用Mushtukov等人（2015）的解析等离子体密度与速度剖面；实现自适应步长算法；在散射区域基底部（热点）进行特殊边界处理；考察光子-表面相互作用的几何反射与热化两种情形；在一定范围X射线光度下生成角分辨谱，考察光度与观测角θ的影响。

Conclusion: 作者支持的物理结论是：所发展的相对论蒙特卡罗辐射转移框架能定量再现CRSF能量与线宽随光度、视角及脉冲相位的变化，且对GX 304-1的验证支持侧向观测几何。对X射线偏振测量、辐射转移等方法的扩展被认为可提供可检验预言，但摘要未直接将其联系到脉冲星、磁星或FRB，相关可转移性根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: Numerical and analytical calculations are powerful tools for studying the accretion dynamics of X-ray pulsars (XRPs), yet translating their results into quantitative spectral predictions remains difficult. Consequently, dedicated radiative transfer simulations are essential for probing the physics near the neutron star surface and interpreting observational data. In this work, we present a fully relativistic Monte Carlo radiative transfer code developed to investigate the origin of Cyclotron Resonant Scattering Features (CRSFs). To model the accretion environment, we adopted the analytical plasma-density and velocity profiles of Mushtukov et al. (2015), which account for the effects of radiation pressure on the flow. To ensure accuracy in regions of rapid density variation, we implemented an adaptive step-size algorithm. Particular care was given to the boundary treatment at the base of the scattering region (the hot spot). We examined two scenarios for photon-surface interactions: (1) Geometric Reflection, where photons are reflected elastically, preserving their energy; and (2) Thermalization, where photons are instantaneously absorbed and re-emitted, with their energies resampled from the blackbody distribution and directions randomized. We generate angle-resolved spectra across a range of X-ray luminosities to examine the impact of luminosity and viewing angle θ (with respect to the magnetic field). Our results successfully reproduce the observed correlations between the luminosity and the CRSF centroid energy E_CRSF and line width σ_CRSF: both quantities correlate positively with luminosity. Furthermore, they exhibit strong variability over the pulse phase, with E_CRSF scaling negatively with cosθ and σ_CRSF positively. Finally, we validate our framework against the XRP GX 304-1. Our model successfully reproduces the observed spectral variability over an order of magnitude in luminosity, consistent with an edge-on viewing geometry. Looking ahead, extending this framework to include polarization-dependent scattering will yield testable predictions for the new generation of X-ray polarimetry missions.

</details>

Abstract: Neutron star low-mass X-ray binaries, in particular those in quiescence, were intensively used, over the past 15 years to determine the neutron star masses and radii by spectroscopy of the NS surface thermal emission. However, over time, a series of potential sources of systematic biases on these measurements were put forth in the literature. This is because the spectroscopic method required making assumptions. Among the most critical ones are the assumptions of a non-rotating neutron star, and of a neutron star surface emitting uniformly. It was shown that these can cause biases on the radius by as much as ∼ 40%. Consequently, these M-R measurements from low-mass X-ray binaries were supplanted by those from the pulse profile modelling of millisecond pulsars (e.g., with NICER data). However, I will show that the aforementioned assumptions are no longer necessary. Thanks to new tools permitting realistic modelling., we can now control the related systematics, ensuring an unbiased measurement of the radius from low-mass X-ray binaries. I will present the first results that will bring back quiescence low-mass X-ray binaries to the forefront of mass-radius measurements and constraints on the dense matter equation of state.
