Result: 三种方法一致表明Her X-1的超轨道周期存在长期下降，速率约为-10^{-5} s s^{-1}。在此长期趋势上叠加了额外的短时标变化。

Abstract: Her X-1 is an intermediate (or low) mass X-ray binary pulsar that exhibits a ∼35-day super-orbital modulation whose origin-disc precession or neutron-star free precession-remains debated. Earlier work reported stochastic variations without a clear long-term trend. Using ∼50 years of data from CGRO/BATSE, RXTE/ASM, SWIFT/BAT, FERMI/GBM, and MAXI/GSC supplemented with archival measurements, we studied the long-term evolution of this modulation. Three methods are applied: epoch-folding searches for constant periods, χ^2 maximisation in the period─period-derivative space, and timing of main-on peaks via an O-C analysis. All approaches consistently show a long term decrease in the super-orbital period at a rate of ∼ -10^-5 s s^-1, with additional short-timescale variabilities superposed on this trend. The possible mechanisms for the observed decay of the super-orbital period will be discussed.

</details>


### [75] [Cyclotron lines in subcritical X-ray pulsars: Examining their variability with luminosity and pulse phase]()
*Fotiadis, Prodromos,Kylafis, Nikolaos,Loudas, Nick,Trümper, Joachim E.*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 本文通过相对论性Monte Carlo辐射转移模拟，研究了亚临界吸积X射线脉冲星中CRSF随光度与观测角度的变化，成功重现E_CRSF和σ_CRSF与光度的正相关，并用XRP GX 304-1的观测数据验证了模型。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: CRSF是探测中子星表面附近磁场强度的唯一直接诊断手段，但其解释具有挑战性，因为观测显示谱线特征强烈依赖于X射线光度L和脉冲相位，尤其是在低光度XRP中L与E_CRSF及σ_CRSF存在正相关。本文旨在考察驱动这些趋势背后的物理机制。

Method: 采用Mushtukov等人（2015）提出的解析等离子体密度和速度分布以考虑辐射压力的作用，并开发了一台采用自适应步长算法的相对论性Monte Carlo辐射转移代码。通过改变吸积光度进行详细计算以获取角分辨光谱，探讨吸积漏斗内共振散射和相对论性等离子体运动对CRSF演化的影响。

Result: 模拟揭示涌现光谱呈现显著且不对称的CRSF并带有宽的蓝翼；谱线相对经典回旋能量系统性红移，且该红移在更高光度和更大观测角度下减小。模型成功重现E_CRSF和σ_CRSF与光度的正相关；两者的绝对值对观测角度θ敏感，E_CRSF与cosθ呈负相关、σ_CRSF与cosθ呈正相关，因此预测E_CRSF与σ_CRSF之间存在反相关。使用XRP GX 304-1数据，对于吸积漏斗主要为侧视几何的情形，模型成功重现了近一个数量级光度范围内的CRSF变化。

Conclusion: 作者认为，所提出的物理框架（包含辐射压力效应的解析流线分布及其中的共振散射与相对论性运动的Monte Carlo模拟）能够解释亚临界XRP中观测到的CRSF随光度与观测角度的变化，并预测未来的相位分辨观测将揭示E_CRSF与σ_CRSF之间的反相关。

Abstract: Cyclotron Resonant Scattering Features (CRSFs) in the spectra of accreting X-ray pulsars (XRPs) are the only direct diagnostic of the magnetic-field strength near the neutron-star surface, and thus, they are essential for probing the physics of these extreme environments. However, their interpretation remains a challenging task as observations have shown that in many cases the line features depend strongly on the X-ray luminosity L and the pulse phase. Of particular interest is the positive correlation observed in low-luminosity XRPs between L and both centroid energy of the CRSF (E_CRSF) and line width (σ_CRSF). In this work, we examine the physical mechanisms driving these trends, specifically the roles of resonant scattering and relativistic plasma motion within the accretion funnel. We adopt the analytical plasma-density and velocity profiles proposed by Mushtukov et al. (2015) to account for the effects of radiation pressure on the flow. To test whether this framework can explain the observed CRSF variability in subcritical XRPs, we developed a relativistic Monte Carlo radiative transfer code featuring an adaptive step-size algorithm. We performed detailed calculations to obtain angle-resolved spectra, varying the accretion luminosity to explore the resulting evolution of the CRSF properties. Our simulations reveal that the emergent spectra exhibit prominent, asymmetric CRSFs accompanied by a broad blue wing. The simulated lines are systematically redshifted relative to the classical cyclotron energy, with the magnitude of this redshift decreasing at higher luminosities and larger viewing angles with respect to the magnetic field. We successfully reproduce the observed positive correlation of E_CRSF and σ_CRSF with luminosity. Crucially, we find that the absolute values of these quantities are sensitive to the viewing angle θ, with E_CRSF and σ_CRSF exhibiting negative and positive correlations with cosθ, respectively. Therefore, we predict that future phase-resolved observations will reveal an anti-correlation between E_CRSF and σ_CRSF. To validate the model, we performed a consistency check using observational data of the XRP GX 304-1, adopting physical parameters appropriate for this system. The model successfully reproduces the observed CRSF variability over nearly an order of magnitude in luminosity for geometries in which the accretion funnel is predominantly viewed edge-on.

</details>

Conclusion: 作者基于该暴持续时间短、能谱较软且位置与若干磁星一致，得出结论：GRB 260805D很可能是一次磁星爆发。部分内容为谨慎推断（“likely”）。关于其与脉冲星、磁星爆发及快速射电暴（FRB）的更广泛物理关联，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: SVOM/GRM detected GRB 260805D during the routine ground search at 2026-08-05T14:52:21.700 (T0), which is also detected by Fermi/GBM (TrigNum 807634346). With the event-by-event data downloaded through the X-band ground station, the GRM light curve shows that this burst mainly consists of two pulses with a T90 of 0.10 +0.03/-0.01 s in the 15-5000 keV band. The SVOM/GRM light curve can be found here: https://www.bursthub.cn/gcn/2026/svgrb260805D.png In addition, the position of this burst, according to Fermi/GBM localization (RA = 284.2, Dec = -32.2, err = 34.7 degs), is located at about 68.9 degrees from the SVOM optical axis, which is outside the ECLAIRs field of view. Triangulation between SVOM/GRM and Fermi/GBM (3 sigma): https://www.bursthub.cn/gcn/2026/svgrb260805D_triangulation.png Considering the short duration and soft spectrum of this burst, and location consistent with several magnetars, it is likely a magnetar burst. The Space Variable Objects Monitor (SVOM) is a China-France joint mission led by the Chinese National Space Administration (CNSA, China), National Center for Space Studies (CNES, France) and the Chinese Academy of Sciences (CAS, China), which is dedicated to observing gamma-ray bursts and other transient phenomena in the energetic universe. GRM is developed by the Institute of High Energy Physics (IHEP) of CAS. The SVOM/GRM point of contact for this burst is: Jin-Peng Zhang (IHEP) (zhangjinpeng@ihep.ac.cn)

### [77] [Born to be recycled: a comprehensive population synthesis of the Galactic millisecond pulsars]()
*Sautron, Mattéo,Pétri, Jérôme,Mitra, Dipanjan,Dupuy--Junet, Adélie,Pietrin, Marie-Eloïse*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 作者通过种群合成算法（结合SEVN与自研发的脉冲星演化代码）模拟双星中再循环脉冲星的形成，结果显示模拟能很好重现FAST GPPS、PMPS和Fermi巡天观测到的射电及伽马射线脉冲星种群，并预言4FGL中未识别脉冲星应少于220颗。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 毫秒脉冲星是最古老但转速最快的脉冲星，为解释其形成提出了脉冲星再循环假说。作者旨在检验大多数观测到的再循环脉冲星是否通过吸积机制形成，并获取难以通过观测得到的关于其性质的统计量，同时对未来巡天做出预测。

Method: 摘要所述方法为：开发针对双星系统脉冲星的种群合成算法；使用代码SEVN考虑所有双星过程，并用自研代码自洽演化每颗脉冲星，计入无力磁层长期演化、磁场衰减、引力制动与空间演化。每颗脉冲星生于带有主序伴星的双星中并演化至今。射电与伽马射线发射位置分别采用极冠几何模型与带状风模型建模。

Result: 模拟似乎能很好地再现FAST GPPS、PMPS、Fermi巡天中观测到的射电和伽马射线脉冲星种群；发现4FGL目录中未识别脉冲星应少于220颗；观测再循环脉冲星似乎需要较高的视角值，且难以观测自转轴与磁轴对准的再循环脉冲星；双星系统中仅约8.10^-3 %的ONeWDs似乎通过吸积致坍缩对轻度再循环脉冲星种群有贡献。

Conclusion: 作者支持双星中脉冲星再循环（吸积致加速）假说，认为该机制能解释大部分观测到的再循环脉冲星种群，并使合成模拟重现现有巡天结果。据此得出对未来巡天的预测，并指出轻再循环脉冲星仅由少数ONeWD双星系统经吸积致坍缩形成。根据摘要无法判断对脉冲星计时、自转减慢转矩变化或磁星/FRB联系的具体结论。

Abstract: Millisecond pulsars (MSPs) are the oldest but fastest pulsars known to date, the first one, PSR B1937+21 was discovered in 1982. To explain how these pulsars could be formed, a new hypothesis was formulated: the recycling of pulsars, i.e the fact that a pulsar could accrete matter from a companion and be spun up. In this work, we developed a population synthesis algorithm for pulsars which belong to a binary, in order to check whether most of the observed recycled pulsars were formed via an accretion mechanism and derive statistics about their properties, that are difficult to obtain through observations. We also make predictions for future surveys. Toward the presented objectives, we use the code Stellar EVolution for N-body (SEVN) to take into account all the binary processes and our own code to evolve each pulsar self-consistently by taking into account the secular evolution of a force-free magnetosphere, the magnetic field decay, gravitational braking and spatial evolution. Each pulsar is born in binary with a main sequence companion, and evolve to present time. The radio and gamma-ray emission locations were modeled by the polar cap geometry and striped wind model, respectively. Our simulations seem to reproduce well the population of radio and gamma-ray pulsars observed in the selected surveys (FAST GPPS, PMPS, Fermi). We also found that there should be less than 220 unidentified pulsars in the Fourth Fermi-LAT catalogue of gamma-ray sources (4FGL). High values of the viewing angle seem to be needed to be able to observe the recycled pulsars, and it also seems difficult to observe recycled pulsars with an aligned rotation axis and magnetic axis. We find that only a small fraction, approximately 8.10 ^-3 % of oxygen-neon white dwarfs (ONeWDs) in binary systems appear to contribute to the population of mildly recycled pulsars through accretion-induced collapse.

</details>

Abstract: In the 4FGL-DR4 point-source catalog of the Large Area Telescope (LAT) onboard NASA's Fermi Gamma-ray Observatory (Fermi-LAT), around a third of the sources are still unidentified (unIDs). In this work, we perform a detailed study of one of them, namely 4FGL J2112.5-3043. Only gamma-ray emission has been detected from this unidentified source, with no counterpart observed at any other wavelength as of today. Together with its high detection significance, this makes 4FGL J2112.5-3043 a particularly compelling target for further investigation. The results of our spectral and spatial analyses show that the source photon spectrum is better described with a subexponential cutoff power-law spectral model, with no significant flux variability over time, and a morphology consistent with being a point-like source. We investigate and discuss the characterized emission within the context of both conventional and exotic astrophysics, namely a pulsar origin or potential dark matter (DM) annihilations in a nearby Galactic subhalo. Although our results are inconclusive and neither confirm a DM origin nor firmly establish an astrophysical nature, we find a spectral preference for the <mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>b</mml:mi><mml:mover><mml:mi>b</mml:mi><mml:mo>̄</mml:mo></mml:mover></mml:mrow></mml:math> and <mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>c</mml:mi><mml:mover><mml:mi>c</mml:mi><mml:mo>̄</mml:mo></mml:mover></mml:mrow></mml:math> DM annihilation channels over a pulsar origin, thus making this unID a particularly intriguing candidate for next multiwavelength observations.

### [79] [Results and outlooks of the XL-Calibur mission]()

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: XL-Calibur旨在通过硬X射线（∼15-80 keV，核心测量∼19-64 keV）偏振测量提供对高能天体物理源的偏振观测，并与IXPE的软X射线（2-8 keV）测量互补，从而拓展对产生等天体发射机制的偏振认识。

Result: 2024年飞行中成功观测了Crab系统（脉冲星和星云）与Cygnus X-1（X射线双星），为它们的硬X射线发射（∼19-64 keV）提供了迄今最精确的约束，这些结果与IXPE的软X射线范围（2-8 keV）互补。文中给出2024年飞行结果概览及深入的后续分析。有效载荷正为2027年12月南极飞行做准备，该飞行可持续数周，可观测南半球天体。

Conclusion: XL-Calibur已证明可对硬X射线波段（∼19-64 keV）的天体提供迄今最精确的偏振约束，补充了IXPE软X射线观测的空白。未来南极飞行将依据Swift BAT或MAXI的同期监测选择观测目标，包括吸积中子星、X射线双星和耀变体。摘要未将其结果直接联系到脉冲星、磁星或FRB主题。

Abstract: XL-Calibur is a balloon-borne hard-X-ray polarimeter sensitive in the ∼15-80 keV energy range. The focusing telescope determines polarization by using the angular asymmetry of Compton events, scattering from a passive beryllium rod into a circumjacent CZT detector. In the summer of 2024, XL-Calibur was successfully launched from the Esrange Space Center, Sweden. Conducting measurements from ∼40 km altitude, the payload landed safely near Kugluktuk, northern Canada, approximately five days later. Both the Crab system (pulsar and nebula) and Cygnus X-1 (X-ray binary) were successfully observed, providing the most precise constraints for their hard X-ray emission (∼19-64 keV) to date, complementary to the soft-X-ray range of IXPE (2-8 keV). Data has been shared with the community through HEASARC. Here, we give an overview of the XL-Calibur 2024 flight results, including in-depth follow-up analyses. Upcoming plans are also discussed, as the payload is now being prepared for an Antarctic flight, scheduled for December 2027. Such a flight can last several weeks and give access to southern-hemisphere sources. Targets will be selected for observation based on contemporaneous monitoring with Swift BAT or MAXI and include accreting neutron stars, X-ray binaries and blazars.

</details>

Abstract: Vela X-1 is an archetypal high-mass X-ray binary pulsar in which strong X-ray irradiation from the neutron star significantly affects the companion's stellar wind. A smooth, isotropic wind cannot explain the observed variability in the system, especially the orbital variation in the absorption column density along the sight line, suggesting the presence of additional structures such as photoionisation or accretion wakes. Furthermore, the source intensity exhibits strong variability from orbit to orbit. India's first dedicated X-ray polarimetry mission, XPoSat, was launched on 1 January 2024 aboard PSLV-C58. The mission carries two payloads: the primary Polarimeter Instrument in X-rays (POLIX), operating in the 8.0-30.0 keV band to measure X-ray polarization, and the secondary X-ray Spectroscopy and Timing (XSPECT) instrument, covering the 0.8-15.0 keV energy range. XSPECT has so far observed Vela X-1 during two epochs, comprising five consecutive out-of-eclipse observations and one full orbital cycle. Using these data, we perform a detailed timing and spectroscopic study of Vela X-1 on orbital-period timescales. We find that the variability observed over the orbital period is driven by a comparable contribution from clumpy stellar winds, intrinsic source variability, and accretion/photoionization wake structures. Furthermore, our results confirm that the wake structure has variability at shorter time scales.

Abstract: The recycling scenario, proposed decades ago to explain the formation of millisecond pulsars through a Gyr-long accretion phase, was observationally confirmed by the discovery of the first accreting millisecond pulsar. The link between accretion-powered and rotation-powered pulsars was further highlighted by the identification of transitional millisecond pulsars, which can swing between these two regimes on remarkably short timescales. Despite this progress, our understanding of the physical mechanisms governing these systems remains incomplete. In this talk, I will discuss recent observational results that explore the boundary between accreting and transitional systems, focusing on the detection of coherent X-ray pulsations at low luminosities during the late stages of accretion outbursts. While only one system has so far been observed to clearly switch between rotation-powered and accretion-powered states, these findings provide valuable clues on how accretion may persist or be intermittently halted as systems approach quiescence. I will place these results in the broader context of multi-band observations, which are revealing a more complex picture, partially confirming theoretical expectations while challenging others, including the notion of a sharp boundary between accretion and propeller regimes.

Abstract: This work is based on 2026 May observation of Be-high mass X-ray binary pulsar XTE J1859+083. NuSTAR observed the source during the initial decay phase of the outburst and no significant flux variation was detected throughout the observation. Coherent pulsations were detected in the 3-60 keV energy band, yielding a neutron-star spin period of 9.795566(2) s at MJD 61176. The pulse profile had a complex energy-dependent morphology, characterized by two peaks, a narrow one ~ 0.2 and a broad peak ~ 0.6 pulse phase. The relative amplitude of the first peak increased up to about 32 keV and subsequently decreased at higher energies. A weak intermediate peak was detected near pulse phase ~ 0.9, appearing most prominently in 7-45 keV energy band. A modest energy dependence was observed in the pulsed fraction, which increased from approximately 36% in the 3-10 keV range to &gt;= 40% at energies above 15 keV. The phase-averaged spectrum was well described by an absorbed power-law continuum with a high-energy cutoff and a Gaussian component accounting for the Fe-K<SUB>α</SUB> emission line. Phase-resolved spectroscopy revealed no systematic pulse-phase dependence of the spectral parameters. Comparison with the 2015 NuSTAR observation shows no statistically significant differences in the timing or spectral properties. The similarity of the pulse profiles and broadband spectra at comparable flux levels suggests that the source was observed in a similar accretion configuration during the two epochs separated by approximately a decade.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 本文关注IXPE对XRPs偏振观测带来的新认知与未解问题。X射线偏振信息可用于确定系统几何，但IXPE观测揭示XRPs偏振度普遍出人意料地低、显著低于理论预期，且个别源行为特殊，这些新问题构成了本研究探讨的动机。

Method: 摘要说明了利用IXPE对约十几个XRPs的X射线偏振观测，测量其偏振角（PA）和偏振度（PD）随脉冲相位的变化，并采用旋转矢量模型（RVM）对PA的脉冲相位依赖进行建模以确定系统几何。

Result: 报告的主要结果包括：XRPs普遍表现出出人意料的低偏振度且显著低于理论预期；总体上偏振性质随能量的依赖性很小；但有少数XRPs表现出清晰的偏振能量依赖性，在低能和高能之间出现独特的PA摆动，并在脉冲相位上呈现复杂的能量依赖；部分XRPs需要额外的偏振分量来解释其能量依赖的偏振性质，其可能起源位于绝热半径之外。

Conclusion: 作者认为IXPE的偏振观测使多个XRPs的脉冲星几何得以确定，同时揭示了偏振度显著低于理论预期的现象。对表现出能量依赖偏振的个别源，作者提出可能需要额外偏振分量、且其可能起源在绝热半径之外，并将在讨论中处理这些未解问题及所提方案。根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: Observations of accreting X-ray pulsars (XRPs) carried out by the Imaging X-ray Polarimetry Explorer (IXPE) have provided completely new insight into these fascinating objects, thanks to the indispensable information embedded in their linear X-ray polarization. X-ray polarimetry enables us to measure the polarization angle (PA) and degree (PD) as a function of pulse phase for XRPs, which allows for the system geometry to be determined. Additionally, the radiation from XRPs was expected to be highly polarized, with estimates for a PD of up to 80% for favorable orientations. About a dozen XRPs have been observed by IXPE so far, and pulsar geometries have largely been determined by successful modeling of the pulse phase dependence of the PA with the Rotating Vector Model (RVM). Interestingly, IXPE observations of XRPs have unveiled surprisingly low polarization degrees consistently across the board, significantly lower than theoretical expectations. Several individual XRPs have demonstrated particularly peculiar polarization properties, which adds to the pool of new questions raised as a result of the polarimetric information. Overall, the XRPs observed with IXPE show little energy-dependence of their polarization properties. However, a couple of XRPs display clear energy dependence of their polarization, with unique swings in the PA between low and high energies, and complex energy dependencies over pulse phase as well. Some XRPs require additional polarized components in order to explain their energy-dependent polarization properties, with possible origins outside the adiabatic radius. I will discuss the energy dependent polarization properties of these sources, which vary in complexity and require different approaches in order to properly explain their behavior. I will also address unresolved questions and discuss the proposed solutions.

</details>

Conclusion: 该计划通过协调利用伽马射线、X射线和射电设备，展示了如何约束致密环境中的致密天体种群，并讨论了未来十年多信使战略的影响，包括警报驱动的后续观测、跨波段数据共享以及与下一代射电和高能时间域观测台的协同。根据摘要无法判断其对脉冲星、磁星或FRB的具体物理结论。

Abstract: The Galactic Center (GC) hosts a rich population of compact objects and extreme environments that are central to multimessenger astrophysics. An unresolved gamma-ray excess detected by Fermi Gamma-ray Space Telescope has been suggested to originate from a population of millisecond pulsars (MSPs), although alternative interpretations—including dark matter annihilation—remain viable. Identifying and characterizing a GC MSP population would therefore directly connect gamma-ray, radio, and X-ray observations, and clarify the origin of high-energy emission from the inner Galaxy. We present prospects and latest results from a deep radio search for MSPs in the central parsecs of the Galaxy using a uniquely capable large-aperture radio facility optimized for high-frequency pulsar observations. Observing at such high radio frequencies mitigates extreme scattering and dispersion along the GC line of sight, enabling sensitivity to fast-spinning pulsars that have eluded previous surveys. Our effort complements earlier X-ray searches conducted with Chandra X-ray Observatory and directly tests whether a centrally concentrated MSP population can account for the gamma-ray excess. This program illustrates how coordinated exploitation of gamma-ray, X-ray, and radio facilities can constrain compact object populations in dense environments. We discuss implications for multimessenger strategies in the coming decade, including alert-driven follow-up, cross-band data sharing, and synergies with next-generation radio and high-energy observatories aimed at time-domain studies of compact objects in the Galactic nucleus.

</details>

Abstract: Context: Gamma-ray binaries are a rare subclass of high-mass stellar systems composed of a compact object (neutron star or black hole) orbiting an O- or B-type companion and produce broadband, predominantly non-thermal emission from radio to TeV gamma-rays. Only nine such systems are currently confirmed, and the nature of the compact object has been definitively established in only three, leaving their physical and emission mechanisms incompletely understood. As interacting massive binaries containing compact remnants, gamma-ray binaries provide a crucial link between massive stellar multiplicity, high-energy binary interactions, and the formation of double-compact systems that may ultimately become gravitational-wave sources. Objectives: This project develops a systematic, multi-wavelength framework to constrain the emission processes in gamma-ray binaries and to indirectly diagnose the nature of their compact objects. The study combines phase-resolved radio observations from MeerKAT and archival ATCA data with X-ray monitoring from Swift-XRT and GeV measurements from Fermi-LAT. By coupling detailed radio characterisation with multi-wavelength cross-correlation analyses, the project probes the spatial and temporal relationship between particle acceleration sites and emission regions. Results thus far: As a foundation for this broader program, phase-resolved MeerKAT monitoring data of 1FGL J1018.6−5856 and LMC P3 have been analysed. Both systems appear as compact radio sources displaying orbital modulation and non-thermal spectra consistent with synchrotron emission. The radio/X-ray cross-correlation analysis reveals clear coherence between these wavebands. In both systems, the inferred delays show a clear trend: high-energy emission precedes the radio, consistent with synchrotron emission cooling. Together, the spectral and timing behaviour favours a colliding-wind pulsar interpretation for both binaries. These initial results demonstrate how coordinated monitoring can constrain the preferred emission mechanisms and geometry, paving the way to extend the approach to a population study and to link gamma-ray binaries to the evolutionary pathway toward double-compact, gravitational-wave-emitting systems.

Abstract: The Imaging X-ray Polarimetry Explorer (IXPE) provides unique comprehension about polarized x-ray emissions from many astrophysical sources such as radio quiet and radio loud AGNs, galactic black hole binaries and neutron stars. Polarization maps in X-rays of pulsar wind nebulae and supernova remnants have been probed. IXPE has demonstrated the scientific potential of the techniques but revealed some limitations such as, due to the deposition of ions onto the dielectric of Gas Electron Multiplier (GEM) leads towards the decrease in the gas gain. Also, it has a relatively narrow energy band coupled with rapidly declining efficiency. Another limitation of IXPE GPDs was the large dead time (1.1ms), which is incompatible with the brightest sources. Literature showed that 1-cm drift with an 800-mbar dimethyl-ether gas mixture provides the best polarimetric sensitivity. Further optimization of these parameters depends on the mirror's effective area and the energy of x ray source, but no major improvement is expected with current detector design. So, to overcome all these limitations and better performance, we are developing a next generation 3-D photoelectron track polarimeter based on Gridpix detector with multipurpose ASICs from the Medipix collaboration, Timepix3 and TimePix4. Gridpix structure in principle prevents the buildup of charge on the metallic mesh, offering a small diffusion with respect to GEM. Also, with the Timepix3 we can record Time-of-Arrival (ToA) and Time-over-Threshold (ToT) simultaneously in each pixel. They would also solve the issue of dead time for any planned high- throughput optics. In this work Gridpix detector experimentally characterized at the INAF-IAPS laboratory using a novel gas mixture of argon (Ar) and dimethyl ether (DME) (80:20). Combining the argon with the low diffusion and precise tracking capability of DME improves the detector performance. To extract key parameters such as energy spectra, energy resolution, and calibration from raw experimental data demonstrate a good detector response. For the validation of experimental data, a detailed Geant4 simulation is performed. We present the tuning of the simulation to achieve agreement with experimental measurements obtained using a GridPix prototype, highlighting the importance of accurately modeling both the detector response and the reconstruction methods. These results demonstrate the strong potential of the GridPix detector with an Ar:DME gas mixture as a promising candidate for future high-precision X-ray polarimetry missions.

Abstract: TIGERISS, the Trans-Iron Galactic Element Recorder for the International Space Station, is an Ultra-Heavy Galactic Cosmic Ray (UHGCR) detector that will launch to the ISS in August 2027 on SpX-37 with an assignment to Columbus SOX. TIGERISS builds on the TIGER and SuperTIGER mission heritage and will measure the elemental abundances from _5B to _82Pb for 400 MeV/nucleon to 10 GeV/nucleon. With a geometry factor of 1.06 m^2 sr, TIGERISS, in one year of operation, will match the statistics seen by the first SuperTIGER balloon flight without the need for atmospheric corrections. TIGERISS will achieve single-element resolution measurements across this wide range of charge, allowing us to test cosmic-ray origins, including their source environments and acceleration mechanisms. Previous work from SuperTIGER showed that GCR measurements up to _40Zr support a source acceleration model where supernovae in OB associations preferentially accelerate refractory elements that are more readily embedded in interstellar dust grains than volatiles. This injection of the GCR into the ISM appears to follow a charge dependence consistent with their grain sputtering cross sections. However, for Z&gt;40, SuperTIGER saw an enhancement of volatile and dominantly r-process elements which suggests that there may be an additional GCR source or a change in the acceleration model. TIGERISS's measurements will allow us to check SuperTIGER's results and see if that enhancement continues beyond _56Ba, which would allow us to potentially determine if it is from an additional r-process source, a new feature in the acceleration mechanism, or both. With TIGERISS's results covering nearly the entirety of the s-process, r-process, and rp-processes of nucleosynthesis, we will add to the wider multi-messenger effort to determine the relative contributions of supernovae (SN), Neutron Star Merger (NSM) events, and magnetar flares.

Abstract: The Trans-Iron Galactic Element Recorder for the International Space Station (TIGERISS) is a Galactic Cosmic Ray (GCR) NASA Astrophysics Pioneers mission in implementation for an August 2027 launch on SpX-37 to the ISS Columbus External Payload Facility Starboard Overhead X-Direction (SOX). TIGERISS will be the first instrument to measure the GCR elemental abundances from _5B to _82Pb with single element resolution, covering the energy range from ∼400 MeV/nucleon to ∼10 GeV/nucleon using the proven combination of ionization (dE/dx) detectors with acrylic and silica aerogel Cherenkov-light-radiator (∝β) detectors demonstrated by the TIGER and SuperTIGER stratospheric balloon-borne experiments flown from Antarctica. It improves on the predecessor instruments by using silicon strip detectors (SSDs) in place of both scintillating fiber hodoscopes for track reconstruction and large area scintillator detectors for dE/dx measurement and the instrument trigger, providing superior charge resolution (σ_Z &lt; 0.25) and signal linearity over the full dynamic range of the TIGERISS SSDs, which has been demonstrated in CERN beam tests. TIGERISS also improves on the heritage instruments by using silicon photomultipliers (SiPMs) instead of photomultiplier tubes (PMTs) to avoid the need for high voltage (HV) and for the more compact Cherenkov detector readout allowing the maximum instrument geometry within the payload envelope. The detector is comprised of top and bottom pairs of crossed 9x9 SSD layers with aerogel and acrylic Cherenkov detectors between, yielding an instrument geometry factor of 1.06 m^2sr that will allow TIGERISS in one year to observe GCR statistics comparable to those measured in the first 55-day SuperTIGER flight over their common charge range without the systematics associated with atmospheric propagation corrections. With the possibility of extended observations through the end of the ISS, TIGERISS will test models of GCR origins, including their source environments and acceleration mechanisms, and in measuring nearly the entirety of the neutron capture process nucleosynthesis it will contribute to the wider multi-messenger effort to determine the relative contributions of supernovae (SNe), Neutron Star Merger (NSM), and Magnetar Giant Flare (MGF) events.

Conclusion: 作者认为，负引力子质量耦合、DM引起的有效压强平衡修正与正各向异性协同作用，可使奇异夸克星最大质量落入2.5–5 M_⊙质量间隙，从而为GW190814伴星等质量间隙致密天体提供可能的理论描述，且模型满足稳定性、因果及能量条件，在整个参数空间保持物理自洽。根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: We construct a unified framework for anisotropic strange quark stars combining de Rham-Gabadadze-Tolley (dRGT)-like massive gravity with dark matter (DM) introduced via extended gravitational decoupling (EGD), in which both metric potentials are deformed by independent functions ξ(r) and h(r). Two DM density profiles are embedded in the decoupled θ-sector: the Einasto spike and the Bose--Einstein condensate (BEC). The baryonic sector is governed by the MIT bag model (quark-matter radial pressure <mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msubsup><mml:mi>p</mml:mi><mml:mi>q</mml:mi><mml:mi>r</mml:mi></mml:msubsup><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:mrow><mml:mo>(</mml:mo><mml:msub><mml:mrow><mml:mi>∊</mml:mi></mml:mrow><mml:mi>q</mml:mi></mml:msub><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>4</mml:mn><mml:mi>B</mml:mi><mml:mo>)</mml:mo></mml:mrow><mml:mo>/</mml:mo><mml:mn>3</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:math>), while the pressure anisotropy is parametrized by the Horvat-Ilijic-Marunovic (HIM) prescription applied to the total effective radial pressure <mml:math><mml:msubsup><mml:mi>p</mml:mi><mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mi>e</mml:mi></mml:mrow><mml:mi>f</mml:mi><mml:mi>f</mml:mi></mml:mrow><mml:mi>r</mml:mi></mml:msubsup></mml:math>. We show that the cooperative action of the graviton-mass coupling (m<SUP>2</SUP>c<SUB>1</SUB> &lt; 0) and the DM-induced modification of the effective pressure balance (ρ<SUB>E0</SUB> or <mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msub><mml:mi>ρ</mml:mi><mml:mi>s</mml:mi></mml:msub><mml:mo>≳</mml:mo><mml:mn>50</mml:mn><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mrow><mml:mi>MeV</mml:mi><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:msup><mml:mtext>fm</mml:mtext><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>3</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup></mml:mrow></mml:mrow></mml:math>), together with positive anisotropy (β<SUB>anis</SUB> &gt; 0), drives the maximum mass into the mass-gap window 2.5--5 M<SUB>⊙</SUB>. The predicted GW190814 companion radii (12.3--12.8 km) and the PSR J0740+6620 radii (11.6--12.5 km) are consistent with NICER measurements. We verify the Harrison--Zel'dovich--Novikov stability criterion, the Chandrasekhar condition Γ &gt; 4/3, the Herrera cracking condition <mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>|</mml:mo></mml:mrow><mml:msubsup><mml:mi>v</mml:mi><mml:mi>t</mml:mi><mml:mn>2</mml:mn></mml:msubsup><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:msubsup><mml:mi>v</mml:mi><mml:mi>r</mml:mi><mml:mn>2</mml:mn></mml:msubsup><mml:mrow><mml:mo>|</mml:mo><mml:mo>&lt;=</mml:mo><mml:mn>1</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:mrow></mml:math>, causality (<mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msubsup><mml:mi>v</mml:mi><mml:mi>r</mml:mi><mml:mn>2</mml:mn></mml:msubsup><mml:mo>,</mml:mo><mml:msubsup><mml:mi>v</mml:mi><mml:mi>t</mml:mi><mml:mn>2</mml:mn></mml:msubsup><mml:mo>&lt;=</mml:mo><mml:mn>1</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:math>) and all dominant energy conditions. We observe that the Buchdahl compactness bound 2M/R &lt; 8/9 employed here is the standard GR result. In the present dRGT-like massive gravity framework, the bound receives corrections that we estimate but do not derive in full generality, confirming physical self-consistency throughout the parameter space.

Conclusion: 作者认为该类Swift机动性与宽带、大有效面积及自主瞬变探测相结合，将使EXPO能自主探测、定位并表征瞬变事件的X射线偏振特性，从而在宽带能段更深入理解宇宙中最极端的物理环境。根据摘要无法判断该任务在脉冲星或快速射电暴方面的具体直接联系。

Abstract: The Imaging X-ray Polarimetry Explorer (IXPE) has opened a new window in high-energy astrophysics, demonstrating the unique diagnostic power of spatially resolved X-ray spectro-polarimetry. However, its pioneering observations have also highlighted the need for broader energy coverage and faster response times to fully exploit this observational technique. We present the Enhanced X-ray Polarimetry Observatory (EXPO), a mission concept proposed for the ESA M8 launch opportunity. EXPO is designed to extend the window of spatially resolved X-ray polarimetry into the 2─35 keV energy band with twice better angular resolution and three times effective area in the IXPE energy band, and with three times better angular resolution in NuStar energy band up to 35 keV. . By employing a combination of Low Energy Polarimeters (LEP) and Medium Energy Polarimeters (MEP) based on gas pixel detector technology , EXPO will probe higher energy phenomena inaccessible to current missions, such as cyclotron lines in accreting pulsars, hard X-ray tails in magnetars, the non-thermal emission in young supernova remnants, and expanding sensitively polarimetry of reflection phenomena in molecular clouds, black-hole and neutron star binaries, and radio-quiet AGNs.. A key operational advancement of EXPO is its "Swift-like" agility: unlike IXPE, EXPO features quick slewing and repointing capabilities and flexibility essential for capturing transient phenomena. This allows for the polarimetric study of highly variable sources, including the prompt and afterglow phases of Gamma-Ray Bursts, intermediate bursts of magnetars, and flaring states in blazars and X-ray binaries. To boost the polarimetric science far beyond IXPE and add this time-domain and broad-band science, the payload hosts optics of larger collecting area and better angular resolution and includes a Wide Field Camera for an independent transient detection and spectral imaging capability provided by an on-board Spectroscopic Imaging Camera (SIC). This suite of instruments ensures EXPO can autonomously detect, localize, and characterize the X-ray polarization properties of transient events and in a broad energy band, for a deeper understanding of the most extreme environments in the Universe.

</details>

Abstract: While there are about 100 Galactic sources detected at TeV energies, fewer than ten are associated with binary systems which show regular, persistent, orbitally modulated gamma-ray emission. These sources, known as gamma-ray binaries, consist of a compact object in orbit around an O or Be type star. The non-thermal emission is most likely produced in a shock which forms between the wind from a young pulsar interacting with the stellar wind. The gamma-ray binaries, PSR B1259-63, HESS J0632+057, and LS I +61 303, contain a Be-type star, and the interaction between the pulsar and the Be star's circumstellar disc appears to play a very important role in interpreting the variation in the non-thermal emission produced by these systems. We present our recent results investigating the multi-wavelength behaviour of these Be-gamma-ray binaries.

Abstract: A diverse array of Galactic sources has been suggested to be able to accelerate particles up to the cosmic-ray knee at a few PeV. Popular examples include supernova remnants, compact young star clusters, pulsar wind nebulae, and microquasars. While gamma-ray observations directly trace the maximum energies of the accelerated particles, X-ray synchrotron emission is produced by leptonic populations, and provides a valuable tracer of magnetic field strength, constraining the conditions in the region of acceleration. In this talk, I will give a review of observations of Galactic nonthermal sources with the eROSITA telescope. eROSITA is a soft X-ray telescope which has conducted the most sensitive all-sky survey to date, enabling in particular the study of highly extended emission on the sky. This allows us to investigate, for instance, the interaction of gamma-ray bright SNRs with circumstellar clouds, the magnetic-field properties in pulsar wind nebulae and extended pulsar halos, or the particle population accelerated in young stellar clusters.

Abstract: In the past fifteen years, our understanding of compact stars has significantly advanced through key observations, such as the pulsars PSR J0348+0432 and PSR J0740+6620, whose radius has recently been estimated using NICER and XMM-Newton data, along with the analysis of gravitational waves (GW) events, as GW170817. In this work, we consider polar perturbations, and we calculate the frequency and damping time of the quadrupolar fundamental f-mode of compact objects, constructed using a wide range of model independent hybrid equations of state that include quark matter. We give special attention to the impact of the hadron-quark conversion speed that, in the slow case, gives rise to a branch of slow stable hybrid stars. Moreover, we study the validity of universal relationships proposed in the literature and find out that none of them remains valid when slow stable hybrid stars are taken into account, as has also been shown for the wI-modes and f-modes in blueRaneaSandoval:2022bou, PhysRevD.107.123028 respectively. This fact could constrain the applicability of asteroseismology methods with fundamental modes designed to estimate the properties of pulsating compact objects. We hope that this result could be tested with the start up of the third generation gravitational wave observatories, which might shed some light on the f-mode emission from compact objects.

### [96] [A Microbial Survival Model for the Permanently Shadowed Regions of the Moon Shows Long-Term Survival of Terrestrial Microbial Contamination]()
*Moores, John,Kloos, Jacob,Bischof, Grace,Hayes, Diana,Schuerger, Andrew*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 作者开发的PSR-MSM微生物存活模型表明，月球永久阴影区（PSRs）的低温与避光环境将有效保存枯草芽孢杆菌等陆地微生物污染，在Shackleton和Faustini两陨石坑中需要约30.0和30.8年才能累积-12个对数减少的无菌保证水平，因此PSRs是太阳系中生物杀伤力最低的环境之一。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 随着月球机器人及载人探索的加强，未来数十年将有越来越多微生物被输送到月球表面。月球多数地区会使大多数已知生物失活，而永久阴影区（PSRs）因低温且紫外线辐射少，可能长期保存有活力的生物负荷，因此需要量化PSRs提供的保护程度。

Method: 基于月球微生物存活模型（LMS，Schuerger等人，2019）创建了微生物存活模型（PSR-MSM），该模型考虑真空、温度和紫外线辐射的影响，并评估银河宇宙射线和太阳风粒子等因素可否忽略。采用模式生物枯草芽孢杆菌（b. subtilis），将该模型应用于Artemis任务计划探索的Shackleton和Faustini两陨石坑内部PSRs的环境进行计算。摘要未说明具体观测仪器、频段或时间跨度。

Result: 模型在Shackleton和Faustini两处PSRs分别给出-0.0815和-0.0683个对数每lunation的减少速率，对应分别需要30.0年和30.8年才能累积达到-12个对数减少的单一无菌保证水平；且计算表明银河宇宙射线和太阳风粒子等次要因素即使在上述情况下仍可被忽略。

Conclusion: 作者指出月球PSRs是太阳系中生物杀伤力最低的环境之一，将在数十年内保存有活力的陆地微生物污染。该方法为行星保护与微生物存活建模提供思路，但摘要未涉及脉冲星、磁星或快速射电暴等射电天体内容，依据摘要无法判断其对上述射电天文主题的适用性。

Abstract: With the robotic exploration of the moon intensifying and a new era of human exploration beginning, an increasing number of microorganisms will be transported to the lunar surface in the coming decades. While the conditions on most of the moon will rapidly deactivate most known organisms, the Permanently Shadowed Regions (PSRs) can preserve viable bioburden for long periods of time. This arises because temperatures here are low and little ultraviolet (UV) radiation reaches these areas of the moon since the PSRs are shielded from direct sunlight. To quantify the protection offered by the PSRs, we created a microbial survival model (PSR-MSM) based on the Lunar Microbial Survival Model (LMS, Schuerger et al., 2019). This model considers the effects of vacuum, temperature and UV radiation. Calculations indicate that other lesser factors, such as galactic cosmic rays and solar wind particles may still be neglected even under these circumstances. Our model organism is b. subtilis, a typical microorganism found in space clean room environments. We applied the PSR-MSM to the environment found inside PSRs at two craters targeted for exploration by the Artemis missions, Shackleton and Faustini. The model produced rates of reduction of -0.0815 and -0.0683 logs per lunation, respectively, which implies that it would take 30.0 years for Shackleton and 30.8 years for Faustini to accumulate a single Sterility Assurance Level of -12 logs of reduction. The lunar PSRs are therefore one of the least biocidal environments in the solar system and would preserve viable terrestrial microbial contamination for decades.

</details>

Result: 先前时域巡天搜索已揭示出有前景的超大质量黑洞双星候选体；类星体的随机光变可引入假阳性并阻碍对真正周期性的探测。LSST将探测数百万类星体，为周期性搜索提供宝库。摘要未给出候选体清单或具体探测结果数值。

Conclusion: 在LSST时代可通过周期性类星体搜索探测超大质量黑洞双星，但需应对随机光变造成的假阳性难题。作者提出可与脉冲星计时阵列等引力波实验形成潜在协同。摘要内容为综述展望性质，未提供经验结论；对脉冲星计时阵列仅作为协同讨论对象提及，未给出具体结果。

Abstract: Supermassive black hole binaries naturally form in galaxy mergers. They can be detected as quasars with periodic variability and LSST with the millions of quasars it will detect will provide a treasure trove for quasar periodicity searches. Previous searches in time-domain surveys have revealed promising candidates, but the stochastic variability of quasars can introduce false positives and hinder the detection of genuine periodicity. I will describe the status of previously identified candidates. I will also summarize the main challenges in detecting and confirming binaries in time-domain surveys, as well as recent work to address those. Finally, I will discuss potential synergies with gravitational-wave experiments, like Pulsar Timing Arrays.
